Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFP.jpg)-          Auto – it is similar to scroll, but it adds scrollbars when necessary.The overflow property only works for block elements with a specified height.<html><head><title>Overflow Auto Demo</title><style>div {background-color: lightgray;width: 200px;height: 50px;border: 1px solid black;overflow: auto;}</style></head><body><h2>CSS Overflow Auto</h2><div>Lorem ipsum dolor, sit amet consectetur adipisicing elit. Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFR.jpg)Overflow-x and overflow-y: These two properties are used to specify whether to change the overflow of content just horizontally or vertically or both.<html><head><title>Overflow-x and Overflow-y Demo</title><style>div {background-color: lightgray;width: 200px;height: 50px;border: 1px solid black;overflow-x: hidden; // Hiding horizontal scrollbaroverflow-y: scroll; // Adding vertical scrollbar}</style></head><body><h2>CSS Overflow-x and Overflow-y</h2><div>Lorem ipsum dolor, sit amet consectetur adipisicing elit. Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFS.jpg)Float and clear: float can be used to wrap text around images whereas, clear property specifies what elements can float beside the cleared element and on which side. The float property has the following values.-          Left-          Right-          None-          InheritExample to demonstrate right and left float and the remaining two values left as you to work.<html><head><title>Float Demo</title><style>img.right {float: right;}img.left {float: left;}</style></head><body><h5>Float Right</h5><p><img class="right" src="MS.gif" style="margin-left:15px;">Lorem ipsum dolor, sit amet consectetur adipisicing elit. Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</p><h5>Float Right</h5><p><img class="left" src="MS.gif" style="margin-left:15px;">Lorem ipsum dolor, sit amet consectetur adipisicing elit. Nemo voluptates quae eveniet nam quas temporibus, vitae voluptatum doloremque reprehenderit cumque dignissimos nesciunt omnis consectetur animi. Facilis officiis laboriosam incidunt repellat.</p></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFT.jpg)Float next to each other: The div elements will be displayed on top of each other. If we use float:left the elements float next to each other. Let’s take an example.<html><head><title>Float Next to Each Other Demo</title><style>div {float: left;padding: 10px;}.div1 {background: red;}.div2 {background: cyan;}.div3 {background: blue;}</style></head><body><h2>Float Next To Each Other</h2><div class="div1">First Div</div><div class="div2">Second Div</div><div class="div3">Third Div</div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFU.jpg)The clear property has the following values.-          None – the element is not pushed below left or right floated.-          Left – the element is pushed below left floated elements.-          Right – the element is pushed below right floated elements.-          Both – the element is pushed below both left and right floated elements.-          Inherit – the element inherits the clear value from its parent.<html><head><title>Clear Demo</title><style>.div1 {float: left;padding: 10px;border: 3px solid blue;}.div2 {padding: 10px;border: 3px solid red;}.div3 {float: left;padding: 10px;border: 3px solid blue;}.div4 {padding: 10px;border: 3px solid red;clear: left;}</style></head><body><h2>Without clear</h2><div class="div1">div1</div><div class="div2">div2 - Notice that div2 is after div1 in the HTML code. However, since div1 floats to the left, the text in div2 flows around div1.</div><br><br><h2>With clear</h2><div class="div3">div3</div><div class="div4">div4 - Here, clear: left; moves div4 down below the floating div3\. The value "left" clears elements floated to the left. You can also clear "right" and "both".</div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFV.jpg)The Clearfix Hack: when the floated element is bigger than the containing element, it will overflow outside of its container. To solve this problem, we use clearfix as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Clearfix Demo</title><style>div {border: 3px solid #4CAF50;padding: 5px;}.img1 {float: right;}.img2 {float: right;}.clearfix {overflow: auto;}</style></head><body><h2>Without Clearfix</h2><div><img class="img1" src="Hanumanth Ladwa.png" width="170" height="170">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.</div><h2 style="clear:right">With Clearfix</h2><div class="clearfix"><img class="img2" src="Hanumanth Ladwa.png" width="170" height="170">My name is Hanumanth Ladwa. Completed a Masters degree in Computer Science and taught thousands of students both online and offline. I have more than a decade of teaching experience in various subjects.</div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFW.jpg)Float examples: if we want to add three floating boxes side by side. If the content in the box enlarges the width of each box will break. The box-sizing property allows us to include the padding and border in the box’s total width and height, making sure that the padding stays inside of the box and that it does not break.<html><head><title>Float Example1 Demo</title><style>* {box-sizing: border-box;}.img-container {float: left;width: 33.33%;padding: 5px;}.clearfix::after {content: "";clear: both;display: table;}</style></head><body><h2>Images Side by Side</h2><div class="clearfix"><div class="img-container"><img src="1.jpg" style="width:100%"></div><div class="img-container"><img src="2.jpg" style="width:100%"></div><div class="img-container"><img src="3.jpg" style="width:100%"></div></div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFX.jpg)Inline-block to create navigation links: This is used to display list items horizontally. Let’s have an example that demonstrates inline-block to create navigation links.<html><head><title>Horizontal Navigation Demo</title><style>.nav {background-color: green;}.nav ul {padding: 0;margin: 0;list-style-type: none;text-align:center;}.nav li {display: inline-block;font-size: 20px;padding: 15px;}.nav a {text-decoration: none;padding 8px;color: white;vertical-align: middle;}.nav a:hover{color: red;background-color: white;padding:20px;}</style></head><body><h1>Horizontal Navigation Links</h1><ul class="nav"><li><a href="#home">Home</a></li><li><a href="#about">About Us</a></li><li><a href="#disclaimer">Disclaimer</a></li><li><a href="#contact">Contact Us</a></li></ul></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFY.jpg)Let’s have an example on Website Design<html><head><title>Website Design Demo</title><style>.leftmenu li {list-style-type: none;margin:0;padding:0;width:150px;background-color:lightpink;border: 1px solid black;text-align: center;padding: 8px;}.leftmenu a {text-decoration: none;padding 8px;color: red;display: block;}.nav {background-color: green;}.nav ul {padding: 0;margin: 0;list-style-type: none;text-align:center;}.nav li {display: inline;font-size: 20px;padding: 15px;}.nav a {text-decoration: none;padding 8px;color: white;vertical-align: middle;}.nav a:hover{color: red;background-color: white;padding:20px;}.header, .footer {background-color: gray;color: white;padding: 15px;}</style></head><body><div class="header">Logo<h1><center>Website Header</h1></center></div><ul class="nav"><li><a href="#home">Home</a></li><li><a href="#about">About Us</a></li><li><a href="#disclaimer">Disclaimer</a></li><li><a href="#contact">Contact Us</a></li></ul><br><ul class="leftmenu"><li><a href="#home">Home</a></li><li><a href="#news">News</a></li><li><a href="#contact">About Us</a></li><li><a href="#about">Disclaimer</a></li></ul><br><br><div class="footer">CopyRights Reserved</div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGFZ.jpg)Dropdown Navigation Bar: It is possible to have a dropdown box for images as shown in the following example.<!DOCTYPE html><html><head><title>Image Dropdown Demo</title><style>.dropdown {position: relative;display: inline-block;}.dropdown-content {display: none;position: absolute;background-color: #f9f9f9;min-width: 160px;box-shadow: 0px 5px 10px 0px;z-index: 1;}.dropdown:hover .dropdown-content {display: block;}.desc {padding: 15px;text-align: center;}</style></head><body><h2>Hover Mouse Pointer on Image</h2><div class="dropdown"><img src="1.jpg" width="100" height="50"><div class="dropdown-content"><img src="1.jpg"width="300" height="200"><div class="desc">Beautiful Flower</div></div></div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG0.jpg)Dropdown Navigation Bar: dropdown menu can be added in a navigation bar as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Dropdown Navigation Bar Demo</title><style>ul {list-style-type: none;margin: 0;padding: 0;overflow: hidden;background-color: black;}li {float: left;}li a, .dropbtn {display: inline-block;color: white;text-align: center;padding: 14px 16px;text-decoration: none;}li a:hover, .dropdown:hover .dropbtn {background-color: red;color: black;}li.dropdown {display: inline-block;}.dropdown-content {display: none;position: absolute;background-color: white;min-width: 160px;box-shadow: 0px 5px 10px 0px;z-index: 1;color:red;}.dropdown-content a {color: black;padding: 12px 16px;text-decoration: none;display: block;text-align: left;}.dropdown-content a:hover {background-color: #a1a1a1;}.dropdown:hover .dropdown-content {display: block;}</style></head><body><ul><li><a href="#home">Home</a></li><li><a href="#about">About Us</a></li><li class="dropdown"><a href="javascript:void(0)" class="dropbtn">News</a><div class="dropdown-content"><a href="#">Hollywood</a><a href="#">Bollywood</a><a href="#">Kollywood</a></div></li></ul></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG1.jpg)With CSS it is possible to create an image gallery as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Responsive Image Gallery</title><style>div.gallery {border: 1px solid #aaa;}div.gallery:hover {border: 1px solid #666;}div.gallery img {width: 100%;height: auto;}div.desc {padding: 15px;text-align: center;}* {box-sizing: border-box;}.responsive {padding: 0 6px;float: left;width: 24.99999%;}@media only screen and (max-width: 700px) {.responsive {width: 49.99999%;margin: 6px 0;}}@media only screen and (max-width: 500px) {.responsive {width: 100%;}}.clearfix:after {content: "";display: table;clear: both;}</style></head><body><h2>Responsive Image Gallery</h2><div class="responsive"><div class="gallery"><a target="_blank" href="1.jpg"><img src="1.jpg" width="600" height="400"></a><div class="desc">Beautiful Flowers</div></div></div><div class="responsive"><div class="gallery"><a target="_blank" href="2.jpg"><img src="2.jpg" width="600" height="400"></a><div class="desc">Beautiful Butterflies</div></div></div><div class="responsive"><div class="gallery"><a target="_blank" href="3.jpg"><img src="3.jpg" width="600" height="400"></a><div class="desc">Beautiful Bridge</div></div></div></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG2.jpg)CSS Forms: Forms are improved with CSS as compared to HTML.Styling input fields: as we have already studied about input fields in HTML like, input type text, input type password, input type hidden etc. with all these input fields we can use attributes of CSS. Here in the below code we use width property to determine the width of the field.<html><head><title>Input Field width Demo</title><style>input {width: 100%;}</style></head><body><p>Input Field width Demo</p><form><label for="fname">First Name</label><input type="text" id="fname" name="fname"></form></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG3.jpg)Bordered inputs: we use the border property to change the border size and color and border-radius property to add rounded corners as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Bordered Input Field Demo</title><style>input {width: 100%;padding: 12px 20px;margin: 8px 0;box-sizing: border-box;border: 2px solid red;border-radius: 4px;}</style></head><body><p>Bordered Input Field Demo</p><form><label for="fname">First Name</label><input type="text" id="fname" name="fname"></form></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG4.jpg)Input field background: This property is used to set the background of text input as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Input Background color Demo</title><style>input {width: 100%;padding: 12px 20px;margin: 8px 0;box-sizing: border-box;border: 2px solid red;border-radius: 4px;background-color: green;}</style></head><body><p>Bordered Input Field Demo</p><form><label for="fname">First Name</label><input type="text" id="fname" name="fname"></form></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG5.jpg)Search icon in input field: we can add icon in input text field. We use background-image and background-position properties. Let’s have a look at the following example that includes a magnifying image to make the input field a search box.<html><head><title>Adding Icon to Input field</title><style>input[type=text] {width: 100%;box-sizing: border-box;border: 2px solid #ccc;border-radius: 4px;font-size: 16px;background-color: white;background-image: url('search.png');background-position: 10px 10px;background-repeat: no-repeat;padding: 12px 20px 12px 40px;}</style></head><body><p>Input with icon:</p><form><input type="text" name="search" placeholder="Search.."></form></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG6.jpg)Animated search input: to do the animation with the input field, use transition property as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Animated Input Field</title><style>input {width: 100px;box-sizing: border-box;border: 2px solid #aaa;border-radius: 4px;font-size: 14px;background-color: white;background-image: url('search.png');background-position: 10px 10px;background-repeat: no-repeat;padding: 12px 20px 12px 40px;transition: width 0.4s ease-in-out;}input:focus {width: 100%;}</style></head><body><p>Animated Input Field:</p><form><input type="text" name="search" placeholder="Search.."></form></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG7.jpg)Once we click in the input field, it expands by animating as shown below.![](image_rsrcGG8.jpg)<html><head><title>Input Button Demo</title><style>input[type=submit], input[type=reset] {background-color: green;border: 1px solid black;color: white;padding: 16px 32px;text-decoration: none;margin: 4px 2px;cursor: pointer;}</style></head><body><p>Styled input buttons.</p><input type="submit" value="Submit"><input type="reset" value="Reset"></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGG9.jpg)Let’s create a form that accepts some values from the user in various input fields as shown below.<html><head><title>Sign Up Form Demo</title><style>#aform {color: white;display: table;padding 15px;background-color: #2c8f77;border: solid 2px black;}.row {display: table-row;}.row label {display: table-cell;padding: 2px;text-align: right;}.row input {display: table-cell;padding: 2px;}input[type=submit], input[type=reset] {background-color: green;border: 1px solid black;color: white;padding: 16px 32px;text-decoration: none;margin: 4px 2px;cursor: pointer;}</style></head><body><h2>Sign Up Form</h2><form id="aform" action="" method=""><p><div class="row"><label for="name">Name:</label><input type="text" id="name" name=""><br><br></div><div class="row"><label for="address">Address:</label><input type="text" id="address" name=""><br><br></div><div class="row"><label for="phone">Phone:</label><input type="tel" id="phone" name=""><br><br></div><div class="row"><label for="email">Email:</label><input type="email" id="email" name=""></div></p></form><input type="submit" value="Submit"><input type="reset" value="Reset"></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGA.jpg)CHAPTER 10INTRODUCTION TO JAVASCRIPTIntroduction: What is scripting language?It is a list of commands that are embedded in a web page, interpreted and executed by a certain scripting engine.Scripts are written for various purposes such as automating the processes on a local-computer or to generate web pages on the web. The programming languages in which scripts are written are called scripting languages. Most of the common types of scripting languages are, JavaScript, VBScript, ASP (Active Server Pages), PHP (Hypertext Preprocessor), PERL (Practical Extraction and Reporting Language), JSP (Java Server Pages) etc.Scripting language is classified in to two types,-          Client-side script-          Server-side scriptClient-side script: It enables interaction with the webpage. The client-side scripts are downloaded at the client end and then interpreted and executed by the browser. Note that the client-side scripting is browser dependent.Some sample uses of client-side scripting is listed as below,-          To get data from users’ screen interface or web browser.-          Used in online games.-          Webpage can be customized without reloading the page.- In the user login page, if the credentials of the user are incorrect, the webpage shows an error message on the client computer without submitting it to the server.Server-side script: It enables the completion or carrying out a task at the server end and then sending the result to the client end. In server-side script, the server does all the tasks, so it doesn’t matter which browser is being used at client end. Server-side scripting is used to send the information to the server to process the script at server end.Some sample uses of server-side scripting is listed as below,- Password protection: whenever the user signs up in any webpage, the user name and the password will be saved in the server. Whenever a user wishes to login, the server checks the credentials (username and password) of the user in the server, if it matches then only the user will be allowed to access the server-side resources otherwise, client-side scripting will hold the user back.- Browser customization: browser appearance such as change of browser skins and users’ experience when the user is logged into the server with the specific required credentials. Browsers appear different for different users.- Form processing: whenever we submit an online application, the data inputted in the form will be stored in the database of the server.- Building and displaying pages created from a database: once the data is successfully submitted to the server through online forms, data from the server can be accessed and represented in the form of report, data pages, forms etc.- Dynamically editing, changing or adding content to web pages: at server side at any moment we can edit, add, remove or update content of the webpage.This book is dedicated to JavaScript; hence, we will discuss the JavaScripting language.JavaScript is a popular, easy to learn web-based programming language. It is also used with HTML and CSS to create responsive web pages and websites. JavaScript was developed by Brendan Eich in 1995\. Java and JavaScript both are different languages in concept and design.JavaScript can change the HTML content using one of the HTML methods getElementById(). Let’s take an example to demonstrate the use of this method.<html><head><title>Changing HTML Content Using JavaScript</title></head><body><p id="sample">JavaScript can change HTML content.</p><button type="button" onclick='document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Welcome to the world of JavaScript!">Click Me For Surprise!</button></body></html>In this example, an HTML element with id=”sample” and changes the element content innerHTML to "Welcome to the world of JavaScript!"Output:![](image_rsrcGGB.jpg)Once you click on Click Me For Surprise! Button the content in the HTML changes and looks as shown in the following figure.![](image_rsrcGGC.jpg)Note: JavaScript works fine with both double and single quotes.JavaScript can change HTML attribute values. We can change the attributes of an HTML code; in the following example we will change the src attribute value and change the image.<html><head><title>JavaScript can change HTML attribute values</title></head><body><p>JavaScript can change HTML attribute values.</p><button onclick="document.getElementById('myImage').src='redcar.jpg'">Change the Image</button><img id="myImage" src="bluecar.jpg" style="width:100px"></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGD.jpg)Upon clicking on change the image button we get the following output.![](image_rsrcGGE.jpg)JavaScript can change HTML CSS styles: The following example demonstrates the use of JavaScript can change HTML and CSS styles.<html><head><title>JavaScript can change HTML CSS styles</title></head><body><p id="sample">JavaScript can change the style of an HTML element.</p><button type="button" onclick="document.getElementById('sample').style.color='red'">Change Color</button></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGF.jpg)Upon clicking the Change Color button, JavaScript can change the style of an HTML element text color changes to red as shown in the following output.![](image_rsrcGGG.jpg)JavaScript can hide HTML elements: The property display: none is set to hide an HTML element as shown in the following example,<html><head><title>JavaScript can hide HTML elements</title></head><body><p id="sample">Enter the Name:<input type="text"></p><button type="button" onclick="document.getElementById('sample').style.display='none'">Click Me!</button></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGH.jpg)Upon clicking on Click Me! Button, the text box can be hidden as shown in the following output.![](image_rsrcGGJ.jpg)JavaScript can show HTML elements: The property display: block is set to show an HTML element as shown in the following example,<html><head><title>JavaScript can show HTML elements</title></head><body><p id="sample" style="display:none">Enter the Name:<input type="text"></p><button type="button" onclick="document.getElementById('sample').style.display='block'">Show Me!</button></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGK.jpg)Upon clicking on Show Me! Button, we will be able to see the input box as shown in the following figure.![](image_rsrcGGM.jpg)In HTML, JavaScript code is written between <SCRIPT> and </SCRIPT> tags as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>First JavaScript Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "My First JavaScript Program written in SCRIPT tag";</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGN.jpg)JavaScript function: it is a finite set of lines of code, that will be executed when it is called. These functions will execute when an event occurs, such as button click event, mouse move event, mouse hover event and so on and so forth.JavaScript can be used in the <BODY> or in the <HEAD> or in both as shown in the following examples given below.<html><head><title>JavaScript in Head and Body Demo</title><script>function myFunction1() {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Paragraph changed in head.";}</script></head><body><p id="sample">A Paragraph.</p><script>function myFunction2() {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Paragraph changed in body.";}</script><h2>Demo JavaScript in Head</h2><button type="button" onclick="myFunction1()">Check It</button><h2>Demo JavaScript in Body</h2><button type="button" onclick="myFunction2()">Check It</button></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGP.jpg)JavaScript can also be kept in an external file as shown in the following example.function myFunction() {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Text Changed.";}This function in another notepad saves it with .js extension. This code is written in an external file ExternalScript.js file and can be used in many different webpages. To import this file into the JavaScript code, write the name of the script file in the src attribute of a <SCRIPT> tag as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>External JavaScript Demo</title></head><body><h2>Demo External JavaScript</h2><p id="sample">A Text.</p><button type="button" onclick="myFunction()">Check it</button><script src="ExternalScript.js"></script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGR.jpg)Upon clicking on the Check it button, it gives the following output as shown below.![](image_rsrcGGS.jpg)Note: You can place an external script reference either in <HEAD> or <BODY> tag and the external scripts cannot contain <SCRIPT> tags as shown in the above example.JavaScript Display: There are four possibilities to display in JavaScript as below.-          Display element using innerHTML-          Display output using document.write()-          Display alert box using window.alert()-          Display browser console using console.log()HTML Code to demonstrate innerHTML<html><head><title>innerHTML Demo</title></head><body><p>innerHTML Demo</p><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = 4 + 1;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGT.jpg)HTML Code to demonstrate document.write()<html><head><title>document.write Demo</title></head><body><p>document.write Demo</p><p id="sample"></p><script>document.write(4 + 1);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGU.jpg)HTML Code to demonstrate window.alert()<html><head><title>window.alert Demo</title></head><body><p>window.alert Demo</p><p id="sample"></p><script>window.alert(4 + 1);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGV.jpg)Note: instead of writing window.alert() we can simply write alert().HTML Code to demonstrate console.log()<html><head><title>console.log Demo</title></head><body><p>console.log Demo</p><script>console.log(4 + 1);</script></body></html>Note: Activate debugging by pressing F12 on your keyboard, select Console in the debugger menu, click run again.JavaScript Print: JavaScript doesn’t have either print object or print methods but it has window.print() method in the web browser to print as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>window.print() Demo</title></head><body><h2>The window.print() Method</h2><button onclick="window.print()">Print this page</button></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGW.jpg)Upon clicking on Print this page, the following print preview dialog box appears.![](image_rsrcGGX.jpg)CHAPTER 11STATEMENTSIntroduction: A computer program is a finite set of instructions to be executed by the computer. In a programming language, these instructions are called statements. JavaScript instructions are executed by the interpreter of the browser. And JavaScript statements are made of values, constants, keywords, operators, operands etc. the statements are executed in the order in which they have been written. Each JavaScript statement must be terminated by a semicolon in order to write various statements in the same line.Note: It is recommended to have a semicolon at the end of each statement but it is not mandatory. Set of instructions is sometimes called JavaScript code.Let’s have a JavaScript code to add two numbers<html><head><title>JavaScript Statements Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a, b, c;a = 4; b = 1; c = a + b;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = c;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGY.jpg)Observe that more than one statement is written in a single line separated by a semicolon.JavaScript code blocks: These are the finite set of instructions written within the curly braces {…}. These statements are called compound statements. The purpose of grouping instructions together in a block to perform some specific operation on it. We are familiar with an example to use curly braces in the JavaScript function.JavaScript Keywords: keywords are the reserved words that have fixed meaning to the interpreter of the web browser that cannot be changed by the programmer. These keywords cannot be used as variable names.Some of the keywords are listed in the table below.

| keyword | Description |
| var | It is used to declare a variable. |
| let | It is used to declare a block variable. |
| const | It declares a block constant. |
| if | It is used to check the condition and returns Boolean value (either TRUE or FALSE value). |
| switch | It is used to choose a particular statement out of several alternatives available. |
| for | It is used to repeatedly execute a single statement or set of statements. |
| function | It is used to declare a function |
| return | It is used to exit from a function. |
| try | It is used in error handling. |

JavaScript Syntax: Syntax is a grammar of any programming language. The programmer must follow the rules of the programming language to write the instructions in a specific programming language.JavaScript values: The JavaScript syntax defines two types of values.-          Fixed values-          Variable valuesFixed values: The quantity that does not change during the execution of a program is called fixed value or Literals or Constants.Example: any constant value with or without decimal like, 5, -10, 15.4 etc.String constants written within double or single quotes like, “Hanumanth Ladwa” or ‘Hanumanth Ladwa’Variable values: The quantity that changes during the execution of a program is called variables. These are the named memory locations to store values or constants. To declare the variables, JavaScript uses the keywords var, let and const. And the assignment operator is used to initialize the variables.Example: let a;a=10;Operators: Arithmetic operators +, - , *, / are used to form an expression.Example:let a=2, b=3, c;c=a+b;c=a-b;c=a*b;c=a/b;Expressions: expressions are the combination of operators and operands.Example: operands may consist of only constants or combination of constants and variables or only variables.Example:let a,b,c;c=3*5;c=a+5;c=a+b;c=10+b;var name=“Hanumanth” + “ “ + “Ladwa”; // strings will be concatenatedvar x;const y;are a few examples.Note: if the variables are declared without a value, then the variable will have the value undefined.Comments: Comments are the textual information for the other programmer to understand the purpose of the program. These statements are ignored by the interpreter of the web browser. There are two types of comments in JavaScript.-          Single line comment (//…)-          Multiline comment (/*…*/)Single line comment: This type of comment is written when there is a single line of text.Example: // This is a single line commentMultiline comment: This type of comment is written when there are multiple lines of text.Example: /* This is amultiline comment*/Identifiers: Identifiers are the names given to the programming elements such as variables, labels, functions etc.Rules for declaring identifiers in JavaScript:- The identifier name must begin with a letter (A to Z or a to z), or an underscore (_), or a dollar sign ($).-          Both uppercase and lowercase letters are distinct. Example, sample is different from SAMPLE.-          Keywords cannot be used as an identifier name.-          Identifiers must have reasonable length.-          It must not consist of special characters other than underscore and dollar symbol.Examples: $name_of_the_company, _salary, fees etc.Character set: JavaScript uses the Unicode characters that covers all the characters (A – Z or a – z), punctuations (@, #, !, ?, / “, ‘, etc) and digits (0 - 9).Declaring variables using let: the variables defined with let cannot be redeclared, must be declared before use and they have block scope.Example: let name=”Hanumanth”;let name=”Priyanka”;​// This statement creates errors stating that name has // already been declaredbut with var we can declare the same variable multiple times.Example: var name=”Hanumanth”;var name=”Priyanka”;it has block scope, meaning that the variables declared inside a { and } creates a block and the variables declared with let cannot be accessed whereas, variables declared with var can be accessed outside the block as shown in the following example.{let a=10;}// a cannot be accessed hereSimilarly when used the same variable with var.{var a=10;}// a can be accessed hereIf the variable is redeclared as shown in the example below see what happens.Example 1:var a=10;{var a=5;}// here a is 5Example 2:let a=10;{let a=5;}// here a is 10Declaring variables with const: variables declared with const cannot be redeclared, reassigned and they have block scope.Example: const PI=22/7;PI=3.142;​// This is not allowed, produces an errorIn case of constant arrays,const names = [“Priyanka”,”Sneha”,”Swaroop”];names[0]=”Raj”;​// here you can change an elementnames.push(“Rakshita”);​// you can add an elementbut you cannot reassign the array,const names = [“Priyanka”,”Sneha”,”Swaroop”];names=[“Raj”,”Rakshita”];​// This produces an errordeclaring a variable with const is similar to let when it comes to block scope.Example, const a=10;{const a=20;}// here a is 10Redeclaring a JavaScript var variable is allowed anywhere in a programExample,var a=2;var a=3;a=4;redeclaring an existing var and let variable to const, in the same scope, is not allowed.Example:var a=2;​// Allowedconst a=3;​// Not allowed{let a=2;​// Allowedconst a=3;​// Not allowed}{const a=2;​// Allowedconst a=3;​// Not allowed}Reassigning an existing const variable, in the same scope, is not allowed.Example:const a=2;​// Allowedvar a=3;​// Not allowedlet a=4; ​// Not allowedconst a=5; ​// Not allowed{const a=2;​// Allowedvar a=3;​// Not allowedlet a=4; ​// Not allowedconst a=5; ​// Not allowed}Redeclaring a variable with const, in another scope, or in another block, is allowed.Example:const a=10;​​// Allowed{const a=20;​​// Allowed}{const a=30;​​// Allowed}Const hoisting: variables defined with var are hoisted to the top and can be initialized at any time. The variable can be used before it is declared.Example:Yourname=”Hanumanth”;var Yourname;variables defined with const are also hoisted to the top, but not initialized. Using a const variable before it is declared will result in a ReferenceError. Let’s have a look at programming examples to understand.<html><head><title>JavaScript Hoisting</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>try {alert(Yourname);const Yourname = "Hanumanth";}catch (err) {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = err;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGGZ.jpg)CHAPTER 12OPERATORSIntroduction: an operator is a symbol that tells the compiler what specific mathematical or logical manipulation has to be performed.Arithmetic operators: The operators supported by JavaScript are: +, -, *, /, %, **, ++, --Let us consider, a=10 and b=2 then

| Operator | Description |
| + | Adds two or more operands |
| - | Subtract second operand from the first |
| * | Multiply first and second operand |
| / | Performs integer division and returns quotient |
| % | Performs modular division and returns remainder |
| ** | Performs exponentiation |
| ++ | Increment variable value by 1 |
| -- | decrement variable value by 1 |

The bitwise operators supported by JavaScript are listed in the following table:Let a=10 and b=11

| Operator | Description | Example |
| & | Binary AND return bit 1 if both operands’ bits are 1, otherwise 0. | a&b=0000 1010 |
| &#124; | Binary OR return bit o if both operands bits are 0, otherwise 1. | a&#124;b=0000 1011 |
| ^ | Binary eXclusive OR (XOR) returns bit 0 if there are odd numbers of 1’s. Otherwise 1. | a^b=0000 1110 |
| ~ | Binary negation complements the bits. Converts bits 1 to 0 and vice versa. | ~a=0000 0101 |
| << | Binary left shift operator shifts the number of bits specified onto the left. | a<<2=0010 1000 |
| >> | Binary right shift operator shifts the number of bits specified onto the right. | a>>2=0000 0010 |

Shorthand assignment operators: Shorthand assignment operators are a compact way of writing assignment statements in an expression.The general form of a shorthand assignment operator is,Operand operator=expressionThe following table shows the use of assignment operators:

| Operator | Assignment statements | Shorthand assignment |
| + (plus) | a=a+b | a+=b |
| -  (minus) | a=a-b | a-=b |
| * (multiplication) | a=a*b | a*=b |
| / (integer division) | a=a/b | a/=b |
| % (modular division) | a=a%b | a%=b |
| & (bitwise AND) | a=a&b | a&=b |
| &#124; (bitwise OR) | a=a&#124;b | a&#124;=b |
| ^ (bitwise XOR) | a=a^b | a^=b |
| << (bitwise left shift) | a=a<<b | a<<=b |
| >> (bitwise right shift) | a=a>>b | a>>=b |
| ** (exponent) | a=a**b | a**=b |

Comparison operators: Let us consider, a=10 and b=20 then:

| Operator | Description |
| == | Compares the values of two operands equal or not |
| != | Compares the values of two operands equal or not |
| > | Checks if the value of the operand on the left is greater than the right operand. |
| < | Checks if the value of the operand on the left is less than the right operand. |
| >= | Checks if the value of the operand on the left is greater than or equal to the right operand. |
| <= | Checks if the value of the operand on the left is less than or equal to the right operand. |
| === | Equal value and equal type |
| !== | Not equal value or not equal type |
| ? | Ternary operator |

Logical operators: Let us consider, a=0 and b=10 then:

| Operator | Description |
| && | Logical AND operator checks if both the operands are non-zero then condition becomes true, otherwise false. |
| &#124;&#124; | Logical OR operator checks if both the operands are zero then condition becomes false, otherwise true. |
| ! | Logical NOT operator reverses the state of the operand. If the operand is true then it makes it false and vice versa |

Logical AND: the result of the logical AND is true, when both the operands are true; otherwise the result is false.

| Operand1 | Operand2 | Operand1 && Operand 2 |
| False | False | False |
| False | True | False |
| True | False | False |
| True | True | True |

Logical OR: the result of the logical OR is false, when both the operands are false; otherwise the result is true.

| Operand1 | Operand2 | Operand1&#124;&#124;Operand2 |
| False | False | False |
| False | True | True |
| True | False | True |
| True | True | True |

Logical NOT: the result of the logical NOT is true when the operand is false and vice versa.

| Operand | !Operand |
| False | True |
| True | False |

Type operators:

| Operator | Description |
| typedef | Returns the type of a variable. |
| instanceof | Returns true if an object is an instance of an object type. |

CHAPTER 13DATA TYPESIntroduction: Data type is a type of data that the variable can hold. The computer doesn’t understand the values without data types. JavaScript can hold numbers, strings, objects and more data types.The following example generates an error without the data types.let a=10+”Hanumanth”;in the example above, JavaScript will treat as,let a=”10”+”Hanumanth”;when adding a number and a string, JavaScript will treat the number as a string.JavaScript evaluates expressions from left to right. Different sequences can produce different results as shown in the following example.let a=10+5+”Hanumanth”;returns, 15Hanumanthhere, JavaScript treats 10 and 5 as numbers, until it reaches “Hanumanth”similarly, when we writelet a=”Hanumanth”+10+5returns, Hanumanth105Here, JavaScript encounters a string operand hence all operands are treated as strings.JavaScript data types are Dynamic: Dynamic data types means; the same variable can hold different types of data as shown in the following example.let a;​// a is undefineda=6​// a has a numbera=”Hanumanth”​// a is a stringJavaScript Strings: A string is a sequence of characters enclosed within a pair of double quotation marks or a pair of single quotation marks as shown in the following example.let FirstName=”Hanumanth”;​// Hanumanth written in double quoteslet LastName=’Ladwa’;​// Ladwa written in single quotesif we want to display quotes in the output then we can use them as shown in the following example.let Animal=”That’s an elephant”;​// single quotelet Fastest=”The fastest of animals is ‘leopard’ ”;​// single quoteslet Slowest=’The slowest of animals is “Tortoise” ‘;​//double quotesexample:<html><head><title>Strings Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let Animal="That's an elephant";​// single quotelet Fastest="The fastest of animals is 'leopard' ";​// single quoteslet Slowest='The slowest of animals is "Tortoise" ';​//double quotesdocument.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =Animal + "<br>" +Fastest + "<br>" +Slowest;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH0.jpg)Numbers: JavaScript numbers can be written with or without decimals.Example: let a=23.0;let b=23;the biggest and the smallest numbers can be written in scientific exponential notation.Example: let x=12e3​// this is equivalent to 12000let y=12e-3​// this is equivalent to 0.012Boolean values: Boolean values return either TRUE or FALSE value.Example:let a=10;let b=20;let c=10;(a==c)​​// This returns TRUE(a==b)​// This returns FALSEBoolean values are normally used with conditional (or selection) statements.Arrays: Arrays are a collection of Homogeneous data items under the same name. each array item can be accessed with the help of its index number. Index number starts from 0 to N-1.Example:const names=[“Hanumanth”,”Priyanka”,”Sneha”,”Swaroop”,”Raj”,”Rakshita”];Objects: JavaScript objects are written with curly braces and the object properties are written as name:value separated by commas.Let’s have a look at the following example,<html><head><title>Objects Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Objects Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const person = {firstName : "Hanumanth",lastName  : "Ladwa",Height     : 5.9,complexion  : "Wheat"};document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =person.firstName+" "+person.lastName+ " is " + person.Height + " tall and has "+person.complexion+" complexion ";</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH1.jpg)The typeof operator: we can use typeof operator to find the type of a variable. This operator returns the type of a variable or an expression.<html><head><title>Typeof Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript typeof Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =typeof 5 + "<br>" +typeof 12e3 + "<br>" +typeof "John Doe";</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH2.jpg)Functions: A function is a collection of a finite set of instructions that performs a specific task. The function gets invoked when it is called by its name.Advantages of using functions:- If there is a set of statements to be repeated several times in the program, these statements can be replaced as a function and called (or invoked) whenever and wherever required.- If a task is needed in more than one program or to a group of programmers, a function can be developed and made available to other programs or other programmers.-          Programs can be modularized into functions. Each function can be developed independently, debugged and tested.- If a program is divided into sub-programs, a team of programmers can be formed to solve each subproblem, rather than being solved by a single person.-          If functions are developed in a program, the number of lines of code decreases.Syntax:JavaScript function is defined with the function keyword, followed by function name and parameters in the parentheses. The code to be executed is written in curly braces as shown below.function function_name(parameter 1, parameter 2, …){// Code to be executed is written here}JavaScript functions are similar to procedure or a subroutine in other programming languages.Example:<html><head><title>Function Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Functions</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>function add(a, b) {return a + b;}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = add(4, 2);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH3.jpg)The code inside the curly braces will get executed when it is called. This process of executing code is called function invocation. The code gets executed in one of the following three events.-          When an event occurs such as button click-          When it is invoked from JavaScript code and-          Automatically i.e., self-invoked.When all the instructions written in a JavaScript function executes and encounters a return value, the code stops executing and returns the value back to the calling function. The following program demonstrates how to call a function and how the function code returns a value back to the caller.<html><head><title>Function Call Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>var a = add(4, 2);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;function add(a, b) {return a + b;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH4.jpg)Instead of using variable a in the above program we can also use function call in place of a variable as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Function Call without using variable Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = add(4, 2);function add(a, b) {return a + b;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH5.jpg)Local variables: The variables declared within the function definition are called local variables and they are accessible within the curly braces. It has its scope restricted to the code only, not outside it.Example:<html><head><title>Local Variable Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Functions</h2><p>Outside myNameFunction() MyName is undefined.</p><p id="sample1"></p><p id="sample2"></p><script>myNameFunction();function myNameFunction() {let MyName = "Hanumanth Ladwa";document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML =typeof MyName + " " + MyName;}document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML =typeof MyName;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH6.jpg)Real life objects, properties and methods: In real life, a man is an object.A man has properties like weight, height, complexion etc and the methods like walk(), talk(), watch() etc.![](image_rsrcGH7.jpg)Properties:Man.name=HanumanthMan.weight=73 kgsMan.height=5’.9”Man.complexion=wheat etc.Methods:Man.walk()Man.talk()Man.watch() etc.All men have the same properties but the property values differ from person to person. All men have the same methods, but the methods are perfumed at different times.JavaScript objects: we have studied in the previous sections that the variables can hold values and it holds one value at a time, JavaScript objects are also variables but they hold many values, for example:(Hanumanth, 73, 5.9, wheat) is assigned to a variable named Man.Object properties can be accessed in two ways:objectName.propertyNameORobjectName[“propertyName”]The following example demonstrates the use of multiple values to a JavaScript object.<html><head><title>JavaScript Objects Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Objects Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const man = {name:"Hanumanth", weight:"73kgs", height:"5.9", complexion:"wheat"};document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "A man "+man.name+" has height " + man.height+" feet";</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH8.jpg)Observe that the values of the properties are written as name:value pairs and it is a programming practice to declare objects with the const keyword.Let’s have an example that demonstrates how to access the properties using the second method.<html><head><title>JavaScript Objects Using second method Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Objects Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const man = {name:"Hanumanth", weight:"73kgs", height:"5.9", complexion:"wheat"};document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "A man "+man["name"]+" has height " + man["height"]+" feet";</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGH9.jpg)Objects also have methods. Methods are actions or operations that can be performed on objects.Accessing object methods:Syntax:objectName.methodName()The following example demonstrates accessing object methods.<html><head><title>JavaScript Objects Method Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Methods Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const man = {name:"Hanumanth", weight:"73kgs", height:"5.9", complexion:"wheat", details:function(){return this.name+" "+this.height;}};document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = man.details();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHA.jpg)JavaScript Events: When JavaScript is used in HTML pages, JavaScript can react to these events.HTML events: An HTML event can be something the browser does, or something a user does. Some examples of HTML events are given below.-          An HTML web page has finished loading-          An HTML input field was changed-          An HTML button was clickedJavaScript executes code when events are detected. HTML allows event handler attributes, with JavaScript code, to be added to HTML elements as shown below.<element event=’some JavaScript’>OR<element event=”some JavaScript”>Let’s have a look at the following example that deals with onclick attributes added to a <button> element.<html><head><title>Onclick Button Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="document.getElementById('sample').innerHTML=Date()">Check Date</button><p id="sample"></p></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHB.jpg)Upon click, we get the following output![](image_rsrcGHC.jpg)Here, JavaScript code changes the content of the element with id=”sample”. In the example below, the code changes the content of its own element using this.innerHTML.<html><head><title>Onclick Button this.innerHTML Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="this.innerHTML=Date()">Check Date</button></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHD.jpg)Upon click, we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGHE.jpg)We can also write the JavaScript code to display the date using the function as shown below.<html><head><title>Display Date using function</title><head><body><button onclick="dateToday()">Check Date</button><script>function dateToday() {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = Date();}</script><p id="sample"></p></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHF.jpg)Upon click, we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGHG.jpg)Common HTML events are listed in the table below.

| Event | Description |
| Onchange | An HTML element has been changed. |
| Onclick | The user clicks an HTML element. |
| Onmouseover | The user moves the mouse over an HTML element. |
| Onmouseout | The user moves the mouse away from an HTML element |
| Onkeydown | The user pushes a keyboard key |
| Onload | The browser has finished loading the page. |

Let’s have some programs on these eventsJavaScript code to demonstrate onclick event<html><head><title>Subtraction using onclick Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><button onclick="sub()">Calculate</button><script>let a = 10;let b = 40;let x = b - a;function sub(){document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = x;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHH.jpg)Upon clicking on the Calculate button we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGHJ.jpg)JavaScript code to demonstrate onmouseover event<html><head><title>Addition using onmouseover Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><button onmouseover="add()">Calculate</button><script>let a = 100;let b = 50;let x = a + b;function add(){document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = x;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHK.jpg)Upon mouse over the Calculate button it gives the following output.![](image_rsrcGHM.jpg)JavaScript code to demonstrate onmouseout event<html><head><title>Multiplication using onmouseout Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><button onmouseout="mul()">Calculate</button><script>let a = 10;let b = 50;let x = a * b;function mul(){document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = x;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHN.jpg)Upon clicking on the Calculate button we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGHP.jpg)Number methods and properties: primitive values such as 123 or 3.142 etc cannot have properties and methods because they are not objects. In JavaScript, methods and properties are also available to primitive values because it treats primitive values as objects.The toString method: This method returns a number as a string and it is implemented in the following example.<html><head><title>The toString Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 456;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =a.toString() + "<br>" +(456).toString() + "<br>" +(450 + 6).toString();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHR.jpg)The toExponential() method: This method returns a string with a number rounded and written using exponential notation. Let’s look at an example given below.<html><head><title>The toExponential() Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 4.56;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =a.toExponential() + "<br>" +a.toExponential(3) + "<br>" +a.toExponential(4);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHS.jpg)Note: A parameter defines the number of characters behind the decimal point. And the parameter is optional. If you don’t specify it will not round the number.The toFixed() method: This method is used to return a string with the number written with a specified number of decimals as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The toFixed() Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 4.56;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =a.toFixed(0) + "<br>" +a.toFixed(3) + "<br>" +a.toFixed(4);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHT.jpg)The toPrecision() method: This method returns a string with a number written with a specified length as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The toPrecision() Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 4.56;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =a.toPrecision() + "<br>" +a.toPrecision(3) + "<br>" +a.toPrecision(4);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHU.jpg)The valueOf() method: This method is used to return a number as a number. An example to demonstrate the same in the following example.<html><head><title>The valueOf() Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 4.56;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =a.valueOf() + "<br>" +(4.56).valueOf() + "<br>" +(4+0.56).valueOf();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHV.jpg)Note: The valueOf() method is used to convert number objects to primitive values.Converting variables to numbers: There are three methods to convert variables to numbers.-          The Number() method-          The parseInt() method-          The parseFloat() methodThe Number() method: it returns a number, converted from its argument.Example on the Number() method:<html><head><title>The Number() Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =Number(true) + "<br>" +Number(false) + "<br>" +Number("5") + "<br>" +Number("5.1") + "<br>" +Number("5,1");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHW.jpg)Note: if the number cannot be converted, NaN (Not a Number) is returned.The parseInt() method: parses its argument and returns an integer.Let’s have an example to demonstrate the use of the parseInt() method.<html><head><title>The parseInt() Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =parseInt("-5") + "<br>" +parseInt("-2.3") + "<br>" +parseInt("15") + "<br>" +parseInt("5.1") + "<br>" +parseInt(5,1);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHX.jpg)The parseFloat() method: parses its argument and returns a floating point number.  Let’s have a look at the example given below.<html><head><title>The parseFloat() Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =parseFloat("5") + "<br>" +parseFloat("2.3") + "<br>" +parseFloat("15") + "<br>" +parseFloat("5.1") + "<br>" +parseFloat("5 minutes");parseFloat("minutes 5");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHY.jpg)Number properties:

| Property | Description |
| MAX_VALUE | Returns the largest number possible in JavaScript |
| MIN_VALUE | Returns the smallest number possible in JavaScript |
| POSITIVE_INFINITY | Represents infinity (returned on overflow) |
| NEGATIVE_INFINITY | Represents negative infinity (returned on overflow) |
| NaN | Represents a "Not-a-Number" value |

MAX_VALUE: This property returns the largest number.MIN_VALUE: This property returns the smallest number.Example:<html><head><title>The MAX_VALUE and MIN_VALUE Demo</title></head><body><h4>The max value is:<h4><p id="sample1"></p><h4>The min value is:<h4><p id="sample2"></p><script>let a=Number.MAX_VALUE;let b=Number.MIN_VALUE;document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = a;document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = b;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGHZ.jpg)Positive_infinity: It is returned on overflow.Example:<html><head><title>Positive_infinity Demo</title></head><body><p>POSITIVE_INFINITY</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ0.jpg)Let’s have a look at how JavaScript handles infinity exceptions.<html><head><title>Positive_infinity Exception Demo</title></head><body><p>POSITIVE_INFINITY</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 5/0;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ1.jpg)Negative_infinity: it is returned on overflow.Example:<html><head><title>Negative_infinity Demo</title></head><body><p>NEGATIVE_INFINITY</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = Number.NEGATIVE_INFINITY;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ2.jpg)Let’s have a look at how JavaScript handles negative infinity exceptions.<html><head><title>Negative_infinity Exception Demo</title></head><body><p>NEGATIVE_INFINITY</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = -5/0;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ3.jpg)JavaScript NaN – Not a Number: It is a reserved word specifying that a number is not legal.<html><head><title>NaN Demo</title></head><body><p>Not a Number</p><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = Number.NaN;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ4.jpg)Let’s look at an example that demonstrates Not a Number (NaN).<html><head><title>NaN Example Demo</title></head><body><p>Not a Number</p><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = 500/"Sneha";</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ5.jpg)Number properties cannot be used on variables: The number properties belong to the JavaScript’s number object wrapper called Number. These properties can only be accessed as Number.MAX_VALUE. Example as given below.<html><head><title>Undefined Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 10;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a.MAX_VALUE;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ6.jpg)CHAPTER 14STRINGSIntroduction: A string is a sequence of characters enclosed within a pair of double or single quotation marks. Characters in the quotes may be zero or more.Example: let name=”Hanumanth Ladwa”;ORlet name=’Hanumanth Ladwa’;string length can be determined by built-in length property.Example: let name=”Hanumanth Ladwa”;name.length;​// This will return 15Escape character: These are the characters constants begin with backslash (\). In order to print the single or double quotes or backslash itself, we use the following escape sequence character constants.\’​This prints a single quotation mark.\”​This prints a double quotation mark.\\​This prints a backslash itself.Let’s have a look at the following example that demonstrates all the above three escape sequences.<html><head><title>Use of escape sequences Demo</title><head><body><p id="sample1"></p><p id="sample2"></p><p id="sample3"></p><script>let str1 = "We are glad to announce \"Bonus\" for all employees.";document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = str1;let str2 = "We are glad to announce \'Bonus\' for all employees.";document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = str2;let str3 = "We are glad to announce \\Bonus\\ for all employees.";document.getElementById("sample3").innerHTML = str3;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ7.jpg)Some other escape sequences are written as shown in the table below.

| Escape sequence | Description |
| \b | Backspace |
| \f | Form feed |
| \n | New line |
| \r | Carriage return |
| \t | Horizontal tab space |
| \v | Vertical tab space |

Strings can be objects: Strings are primitive types as we have already seen before.Example: let name=”Hanumanth Ladwa”;But the following statement shows that the string can also be defined as objects with the keyword new operator.let name=new String(“Hanumanth Ladwa”);let’s look at the following example that demonstrate string and object<html><head><title>String Object Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = "Hanumanth Ladwa";        // a is a stringlet b = new String("Hanumanth Ladwa");  // b is an objectdocument.getElementById("sample").innerHTML ="a is a "+typeof a + " and b is an " + typeof b;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ8.jpg)Note: Avoid creating strings as objects because execution speed reduces and this can produce some unexpected results.When we use == operator, equal strings are equal:<html><head><title>When == Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = "Hanumanth Ladwa";let b = new String("Hanumanth Ladwa");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =(a==b);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJ9.jpg)When using the === operator, equal values may not be equal, because it expects equality in both data type and value.<html><head><title>When === Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = "Hanumanth Ladwa";let b = new String("Hanumanth Ladwa");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =(a===b);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJA.jpg)Objects cannot be compared as shown in the following.<html><head><title>When == Object Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = new String("Hanumanth Ladwa");let b = new String("Hanumanth Ladwa");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =(a==b);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJB.jpg)<html><head><title>When === Object Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = new String("Hanumanth Ladwa");let b = new String("Hanumanth Ladwa");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =(a===b);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJC.jpg)Note: two JavaScript objects will always return false values.String Properties and Methods:String length: The length property returns the total number of characters in the string. Code example is already seen in the previous section.slice(): This method extracts a part of a string. This method takes 2 parameters: start position and the end position.syntax:slice(start,end);Example:<html><head><title>Slice Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.slice(11,15);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJD.jpg)If a parameter value is negative then the position is counted from the end of the string.Example:<html><head><title>Negative Parameter Slice Method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.slice(-11,-7);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJE.jpg)If you omit the second parameter, the method will slice out the rest of the string as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Omit second parameter Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.slice(11);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJF.jpg)Or, it can also be used for counting from the end as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Omit second parameter counting from the end Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.slice(-11);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJG.jpg)The substring() method: This method is similar to the slice() method but there is a difference, the substring() method will never receive negative indexes.Syntax:substring(start, end);Let’s have a look at the example shown below.<html><head><title>Substring method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.substring(0,9);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJH.jpg)If you omit the second parameter, substring() will slice out the rest of the string.The substr() method: This method is similar to slice but the difference is that the second parameter is the length of the extracted part.Syntax:substr(start, length);Let’s look at the example given below.<html><head><title>Substr() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.substr(0,9);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJJ.jpg)If you omit the second parameter, substr() will slice out the rest of the string.Let’s have a look at the example given below.<html><head><title>Substr() method Omit second parameter Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.substr(11);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJK.jpg)If the first parameter is negative, the position counts from the end of the string as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Substr() method negative first parameter Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let flower = "Sunflower, Rose, Lilly";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flower.substr(-5);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJM.jpg)The replace() method: String content can be replaced by using the method called replace() as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Replace() method Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="replaceFunction()">Replace</button><p id="sample">Come Office</p><script>function replaceFunction() {let str = document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =str.replace("Office","Home");}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJN.jpg)Upon clicking on the Replace button, the following result will be displayed.![](image_rsrcGJP.jpg)The replace() method is case sensitive. i.e., both uppercase and lowercase letters are distinct. To make the replace() method to be case insensitive, use a regular expression with an /i flag as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Replace() method case insensitive Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="replaceFunction()">Replace</button><p id="sample">Come Office</p><script>function replaceFunction() {let str = document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =str.replace(/Office/i,"Home");}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJR.jpg)Upon clicking on the Replace button, the following result is displayed.![](image_rsrcGJS.jpg)Regular expression /g flag replaces all the matches as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Replace() method global match Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="replaceFunction()">Replace</button><p id="sample">Come Office and Office</p><script>function replaceFunction() {let str = document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =str.replace(/Office/g,"Home");}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJT.jpg)Upon clicking on the Replace button the following result is displayed.![](image_rsrcGJU.jpg)Upper and Lowercase conversions: A string is converted to upper case with toUpperCase() method and to convert lower case with toLowerCase() method.Let’s have an example that demonstrates converting upper-case and lower-case strings.<html><head><title>Uppercase and Lowercase conversion Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="conversionFunction1()">To Uppercase</button><button onclick="conversionFunction2()">To Lowercase</button><p id="sample">Come Office</p><script>function conversionFunction1() {let str = document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =str.toUpperCase();}function conversionFunction2() {let str = document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =str.toLowerCase();}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJV.jpg)When clicked on the To Uppercase button, the following result is shown.![](image_rsrcGJW.jpg)When clicked on the To Lowercase button, the following result is shown.![](image_rsrcGJX.jpg)The concat() method: This method is used to join or combine two strings together. Second string joins at the end of the first string as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Concatenate Strings Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str1 = "Hi";let str2 = "There!";let str3 = str1.concat(" ",str2);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str3;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJY.jpg)The same concatenation operation can also be done with the help of the plus operator as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Concatenate Strings using concat and + Demo</title></head><body><h4>String Concatenation using + operator</h4><p id="sample1"></p><h4>String Concatenation using concat method</h4><p id="sample2"></p><script>let str1="Hi"+" "+"There!";document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = str1;</script><script>let str2 = "Hi".concat(" ","There!");document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = str2;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGJZ.jpg)The trim() method: This method is used to remove blank spaces from both the sides of a string. Let’s have an example that demonstrates the trim() method.<html><head><title>The trim() method demo</title></head><body><button onclick="trimFunction()">Trim it</button><p id="sample"></p><script>function trimFunction() {let str = "     Hi There!     ";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML=str;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK0.jpg)Clicking on the Trim  it button, we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGK1.jpg)String padding: string supports padding at the beginning and the end of a string. Let’s have an example to demonstrate string padding.<html><head><title>String Padding Demo</title></head><body><p>Using padStart() method</p><p id="sample1"></p><p>Using padEnd() method</p><p id="sample2"></p><script>let str = "6";document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = str.padStart(4,0);</script><script>let str1 = "6";document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = str1.padEnd(4,0);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK2.jpg)The charAt() method: This method returns the character at a specified position or index in a string.Example:<html><head><title>The charAt() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>var str = "Hi There!";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.charAt(4);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK3.jpg)The charCodeAt() method: This method returns the Unicode of the character at a specified index in a string.Example:<html><head><title>The charCodeAt() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>var str = "Hi There!";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.charCodeAt(4);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK4.jpg)Property access: It is used to access the element from an index specified in an array.Example:<html><head><title>The property access Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>var str = "Hi There!";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str[4];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK5.jpg)Converting a string to an array: if we want to work with a string as an array, you can convert it to an array using split() method as shown below in the following example.<html><head><title>Converting string into array</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "H,a,n,u,m,a,n,t,h";const a = str.split(",");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a[0];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK6.jpg)Note:-          if the separator is omitted, the returned array will contain the whole string in index [0].-          If the character is “”, the returned array will be an array of single characters.String.indexOf() method: This method returns the index of the first occurrence of a specified text in a string.Example:<html><head><title>The indexOf() method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.indexOf("Home");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK7.jpg)String.lastIndexOf() method: This method is used to return the index of the last occurrence of a specified text in a string.Example:<html><head><title>The lastIndexOf() method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.lastIndexOf("Home");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK8.jpg)Note: Both indexOf() method and lastIndexOf() method return -1 if the text is not found.Both of these methods accept a second parameter as the starting position for the search.Example:<html><head><title>The indexOf() method uses second parameter demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.indexOf("Home",8);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGK9.jpg)The lastIndexOf() method also uses the second parameter. The JavaScript code to find the index of specified text using lastIndexOf() method is left to you as Home Work.String.search() method: This method searches for a specified value and returns the position of the match as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The String.search() method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.search("Home");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKA.jpg)Notice that both indexOf() and search() methods are giving the same result. Are they the same? No, there is a difference in them. The search() method cannot take a second start position argument whereas, indexOf() method cannot take regular expressions.String.match() method: This method is used to search a string for a match against a regular expression, and returns the matches as an array object.Example:<html><head><title>The String.match() method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.match(/Home/g);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKB.jpg)Note: if the regular expression does not include the g modifier to perform a global search, the match() method will return only the first match in the string.In order to perform a global, case insensitive search for the specific string we use the following code.<html><head><title>The String.match() case insensitive method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet HOME";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.match(/Home/gi);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKC.jpg)String.includes() method: This method returns true if a string contains a specified value.Syntax:string.includes(searchvalue, start);where searchvalue is the string to search for and start is option and its default value is 0\. Start represents the position to start the search. This method returns true if the string contains the value, otherwise false.Examples below demonstrate the same.<html><head><title>The String.includes() method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.includes("Home");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKD.jpg)Let’s have an example that returns false value and it uses start value as shown below.<html><head><title>The String.includes() method uses start value demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.includes("My",3);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKE.jpg)String.startsWith() method: This method returns true if a string begins with a specified value, otherwise false.Syntax:string.startsWith(searchvalue, start);where searchvalue is the string to search for and start is option and its default value is 0\. Start represents the position to start the search. This method returns true if the string contains the value, otherwise false.Example:<html><head><title>The String.startsWith() method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.startsWith("Home");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKF.jpg)String.endsWith() method: This method returns true if a string ends with a specified value, otherwise false.Syntax:string.endswith(searchvalue, length);where, searchvalue is the value to search for.Length is optional and it represents the length to search.Example:<html><head><title>The String.endsWith() method demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "My Home is Sweet Home";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str.endsWith("Home");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKG.jpg)Template literals: templates use back-ticks (` `) rather than the double quotation (“ “) marks to define a string as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Template Literal Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = `Hi There!`;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKH.jpg)Using template literals we can write single and double quotation marks inside a string as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Template Literal single and double quote Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = `Sun rises in the "East"`;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKJ.jpg)Template literals allow multiline strings as shown in the example below.<html><head><title>Template Literal Multiline string Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = `Success comesto thosewho areready tomake sacrifices`;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKK.jpg)Interpolation: Template literals provide an easy way to interpolate variables and expressions into strings.Syntax:${…}Variable substitutions: Template literals allow variables in strings as shown in the example below.<html><head><title>Variable substitution Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let str1 = "to the world of ";let str2 = "JavaScript";let str = `Welcome ${str1}${str2}!`;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKM.jpg)Automatic replacing of variables with real values is called string interpolation.Expression substitution: Template literals also allow expression in strings as shown in the example below.<html><head><title>Expression substitution
