Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>let principal = 100;let time = 2;let rate = 6;let si=`Simple Interest: ${((principal*time*rate)/100).toFixed(2)}`;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = si;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKN.jpg)Automatic replacing of expressions with real values is called string interpolation.CHAPTER 15ARRAYSIntroduction: Arrays are the collection of values of the same type under the single variable. You can access each value by referring to an index number. In JavaScript it is a common practice to declare arrays with the const keyword.Syntax:const array_name=[item1, item2, …];<html><head><title>Arrays Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = family_members;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKP.jpg)Using a new keyword: with the help of a new operator, we create an array dynamically as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Array new Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = new Array("Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = family_members;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKR.jpg)Each element of the array can be accessed with the help of its index number.Example:<html><head><title>Array Accessing element Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = family_members[0];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKS.jpg)We can also change the value of the specific subscript of the array as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Array Changing value Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop"];family_members[1]="Raj";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = family_members;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKT.jpg)Arrays are objects: objects use names to access its members as shown in this example.<html><head><title>Array are objects Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = {firstNiece:"Priyanka", secondNiece:"Sneha", thirdNiece:"Sonia", forthNephew:"Swaroop"};document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = family_members.forthNephew;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKU.jpg)Array elements can be objects: arrays can have different types because JavaScript arrays are kinds of objects.Array properties and methods:The length property: This property returns the number of elements.Example:<html><head><title>Array length Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = family_members.length;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKV.jpg)With the help of length property we can access the last array element as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Accessing array last element Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = family_members[family_members.length-1];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKW.jpg)Looping array elements: The best way to display all the array elements is through the for loop.Example:<html><head><title>Accessing Array elements using for loop</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop"];let l = family_members.length;let str = "<ul>";for (let i = 0; i < l; i++){str += "<li>" + family_members[i] + "</li>";}str += "</ul>";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKX.jpg)Instead of a for loop we can also use Array.forEach() function as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Accessing Array elements using forEach() function</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia", "Swaroop"];let l = family_members.length;let str = "<ul>";family_members.forEach(arrayFunction);str += "</ul>";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;function arrayFunction(value){str += "<li>" + value + "</li>";}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKY.jpg)Adding array elements: we can add elements to an array using push() method as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Adding array element Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><button onclick="flowerFunction()">Add Element</button><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;function flowerFunction() {flowers.push("sunflower");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGKZ.jpg)Upon clicking on the Add Element button we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGM0.jpg)New element can also be added to an array using the length property as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Adding array element using length property</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><button onclick="flowerFunction()">Add Element</button><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;function flowerFunction() {flowers[flowers.length]="Sunflower";document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM1.jpg)Upon clicking on the Add Element button, we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGM2.jpg)Adding elements with high indexes can create undefined holes in an array as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Adding array elements with higher indexes</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];flowers[5] = "Sunflower";let l = flowers.length;let str = "";for (i = 0; i < l; i++) {str += flowers[i] + "<br>";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM3.jpg)Arrays and objects: In JavaScript, arrays use numbered indexes whereas, objects use named indexes. We should use objects when we want the element names to be strings(text). We should use arrays when we want the element names to be numbers. JavaScript has a built-in array constructor new Array(). But we can safely use [] instead.Example: const a=new Array(10,20,30,40,50);const a = [10,20,30,40,50];These two different statements create a new array containing 5 numbers.The new keyword can produce an unexpected result as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Unexpected result in array using new</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p>Avoid using new Array().</p><p id="sample"></p><script>var a = new Array(10);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM4.jpg)In order to identify the variable as an array we use Array.isArray() as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Array.isArray() method Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = Array.isArray(flowers);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM5.jpg)Another way to check the variable is array or not using instanceof operator as shown below.<html><head><title>The instanceof operator Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers instanceof Array;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM6.jpg)Array Methods:Converting arrays to strings: The toString() method is used to convert an array to a string of array values.<html><head><title>The toString() method Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Arrays</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.toString();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM7.jpg)Popping and pushing items in an array: Inserting an element into the array is called pushing and deletion of an element from the array is called popping. Inserting an element in an array is done with the push() method and removal of element from an array is done with the pop() method.Example:<html><head><title>Array element Push() and Pop() Demo</title></head><body><h5>JavaScript Array element Push()</h5><p id="sample1"></p><p id="sample2"></p><h5>JavaScript Array element Pop()</h5><p id="sample3"></p><p id="sample4"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = flowers;flowers.push("Sunflower");document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = flowers;document.getElementById("sample3").innerHTML = flowers;flowers.pop();document.getElementById("sample4").innerHTML = flowers;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM8.jpg)Shifting elements: The shift() method is used to remove the first element of the array and shifts all other elements to a lower index.Example:<html><head><title>Array element shift() method Demo</title></head><body><h5>JavaScript Array element shift() method</h5><p id="sample1"></p><p id="sample2"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = flowers;flowers.shift();document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = flowers;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGM9.jpg)The unshift() method: This method is used to add a new element to an array at the beginning and unshifts older elements.Example:<html><head><title>Array element unshift() method Demo</title></head><body><h5>JavaScript Array element unshift() method</h5><p id="sample1"></p><p id="sample2"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = flowers;flowers.unshift("Sunflower");document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = flowers;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMA.jpg)The splice() method: This method is used to add new items to an array.<html><head><title>The splice() method Demo</title></head><body><h5>Before inserting</h5><p id="sample1"></p><h5>After inserting</h5><p id="sample2"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = flowers;flowers.splice(1,1,"Sunflower");document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = flowers;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMB.jpg)Note: Here, the first parameter defines the position where new elements should be added and the second parameter defines how many elements should be removed and the rest of the parameters define the new elements to be added.The splice() method is also used to remove elements without leaving holes in the array.<html><head><title>The splice() method to delete an element Demo</title></head><body><h5>The splice() method to delete an element Demo</h5><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;flowers.splice(1,1);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMC.jpg)Merging or concatenating arrays: To combine arrays to create a new array using concat() method is shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The concat() method Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript concat() Method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const myNiece = ["Priyanka", "Sneha", "Sonia"];const myNephew = ["Swaroop", "Raj"];const mySisterChildren = myNiece.concat(myNephew);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = mySisterChildren;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMD.jpg)Slicing an array: This method is used to slice out a piece of an array into a new array as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The slice() method Demo</title></head><body><h2>The slice() Method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];const flower_bunch = flowers.slice(1);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers + "<br><br>" + flower_bunch;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGME.jpg)The slice() method can take two arguments, the method then selects elements from the start argument, and up to but not including the end argument.Example:<html><head><title>The slice() method with two arguments Demo</title></head><body><h2>The slice() Method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Sunflower", "Lotus"];const flower_bunch = flowers.slice(1,3);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers + "<br><br>" + flower_bunch;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMF.jpg)Sorting an array: This method is used to arrange the elements of an array either in ascending order or descending order as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The sort() method Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="flowerFunction()">Sort Ascending</button><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Sunflower", "Lotus"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;function flowerFunction(){flowers.sort();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML=flowers;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMG.jpg)Upon clicking on the Sort Ascending button we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGMH.jpg)Reversing an array: The reverse() method reverses the elements in an array as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The reverse() method Demo</title></head><body><button onclick="flowerFunction()">Reverse it!</button><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Sunflower", "Lotus"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;function flowerFunction(){flowers.sort();flowers.reverse();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML=flowers;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMJ.jpg)Upon click on Reverse it! Button we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGMK.jpg)Numeric sort: by default, sort() method is used to arrange values as strings “20” is bigger than “100”, because “2” is bigger than “1”. This produces an incorrect result when sorting numbers. We can fix this by providing a compare function as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The numeric sort demo</title></head><body><button onclick="numericFunction()">Sort Ascending</button><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [40, 10, 20, 50, 30, 60];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;function numericFunction() {a.sort(function(a, b){return a - b});document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMM.jpg)Upon clicking on the Sort Ascending button we get the result as shown below.![](image_rsrcGMN.jpg)Finding the highest or lowest array value: As there are no built-in functions for finding the max or min value in an array, we can use the index to obtain the highest and lowest values as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The Minimum and Maximum demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><button onclick="minFunction()">Min</button><p id="sample1"></p><button onclick="maxFunction()">Max</button><p id="sample2"></p><script>const a = [40, 10, 20, 50, 30, 60];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;function minFunction() {a.sort(function(a, b){return a - b});document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = a[0];}function maxFunction() {a.sort(function(a, b){return b - a});document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = a[0];}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMP.jpg)Upon clicking on Min and Max buttons we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGMR.jpg)Note: sorting a whole array is a very inefficient method if you only want to find the highest or lowest value.Using Math.min() and Math.max() on an array: Math.min.apply and Math.max.apply is used to find the minimum and maximum number in an array as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The Math.min() and Math.max() demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><button onclick="minFunction()">Min</button><p id="sample1"></p><button onclick="maxFunction()">Max</button><p id="sample2"></p><script>const a = [40, 10, 20, 50, 30, 60];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;function minFunction() {document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = Math.min.apply(null,a);}function maxFunction() {document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = Math.max.apply(null,a);}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMS.jpg)Upon clicking on the Min and Max button we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGMT.jpg)My Min/Max JavaScript Methods:These functions loop through an array comparing each value with the lowest and highest found as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The min() and max() methods demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><p>The highest number is <span id="sample1"></span>.</p><p>The lowest number is <span id="sample2"></span>.</p><script>const a = [40, 10, 20, 50, 30, 60];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML = myArrayMax(a);document.getElementById("sample2").innerHTML = myArrayMin(a);function myArrayMax(arr) {let len = arr.length;let max = -Infinity;while (len--) {if (arr[len] > max) {max = arr[len];}}return max;}function myArrayMin(arr) {let len = arr.length;let min = Infinity;while (len--) {if (arr[len] < min) {min = arr[len];}}return min;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMU.jpg)Sorting object arrays: Even if objects have properties of different data types, the sort() method can be used to sort the array.Example:<html><head><title>Sorting Array Objects</title></head><body><button onclick="arrayFunction()">Sort It!</button><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_members = [{type:"Sneha", year_of_birth:2008},{type:"Priyanka", year_of_birth:2007},{type:"Sonia", year_of_birth:2011}];displayYear();function arrayFunction() {family_members.sort(function(a, b){return a.year_of_birth - b.year_of_birth});displayYear();}function displayYear() {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =family_members[0].type + " " + family_members[0].year_of_birth + "<br>" +family_members[1].type + " " + family_members[1].year_of_birth + "<br>" +family_members[2].type + " " + family_members[2].year_of_birth;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMV.jpg)Array Iteration: Array iteration means accessing each element of the array at least once to perform some operation called traversing. There are several methods on array iteration as listed below.Array.forEach(): This method calls a function once for each array element.Example:<html><head><title>The forEach() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];let str = "";a.forEach(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {str += value + "<br>";}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMW.jpg)Note that the function takes three arguments, the item value, the item index and the array itself.Array.map(): This method creates a new array by performing a function on each array element. It does not execute the function for array elements without values. It does not change the original array.Example:<html><head><title>The Array.map() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];const b = a.map(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = b;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {return value * 5;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMX.jpg)Array.filter() method: This method creates a new array with array elements that passes a test.Example:<html><head><title>The Array.filter() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];const b = a.filter(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = b;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {return value > 20;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMY.jpg)Array.reduce() method: This method runs a function on each array element to reduce it to a single value. It works from left to right in an array.Example:<html><head><title>The Array.reduce() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];let sum = a.reduce(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "The sum is "+sum;function arrayFunction(total, value, index, array) {return total+value;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGMZ.jpg)Array.reduceRight() method: This method runs a function on each array element to reduce it to a single value. It works from right to left in an array.Example:<html><head><title>The Array.reduceRight() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];let sum = a.reduceRight(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "The sum is "+sum;function arrayFunction(total, value, index, array) {return total+value;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN0.jpg)Array.every() method: This method checks if all array values pass a test.Example:<html><head><title>The Array.every() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];let b = a.every(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Greater than 20 is "+b;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {return value>20;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN1.jpg)Array.some() method: This method is used to check if some array values pass a test.Example:<html><head><title>The Array.some() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];let b = a.some(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Greater than 20 is "+b;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {return value>20;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN2.jpg)Array.indexOf() method: This method searches an array for an element value and returns its position.Syntax:array.indexOf(item, start);here, item – the item to search for.start – where to start the search. The negative values will start at the given position counting from the end, and search to the end.Example:<html><head><title>The Array.indexOf() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Lotus", "Sunflower"];let p = flowers.indexOf("Lilly")+1;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "The element \"Lilly\" is found at position "+p;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN3.jpg)Note: Array.indexOf() returns -1 if the item is not found. If the element is present more than once, it returns the position of the first occurrence.Array.lastIndexOf() method: This function returns the position of the last occurrence of the specified element.Syntax:array.lastIndexOf(item, start);here, item – the item to search for.start – where to start the search. The negative values will start at the given position counting from the end, and search to the beginning.<html><head><title>The Array.lastIndexOf() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Lotus", "Sunflower"];let p = flowers.lastIndexOf("Lilly")+1;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "The element \"Lilly\" is found at position "+p;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN4.jpg)Array.includes() method: This method is used to check if an element is present in an array.Syntax:Array.includes(search-item);Example:<html><head><title>The Array.includes() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Lotus", "Sunflower"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.includes("Sunflower");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN5.jpg)Array.find() method: This method returns the value of the first array element that passes a test function as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The Array.find() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [30, 40, 20, 10, 50];let b = a.find(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "First number Greater than 20 is "+b;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {return value>20;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN6.jpg)Array.findIndex() method: This method is used to return the index of the first array element that passes a test function as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The Array.findIndex() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [10, 20, 30, 40, 50];let b = a.findIndex(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "First number Greater than 20 is "+b;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {return value>20;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN7.jpg)Array.from() method: This method returns an array object from any object with a length property or any iterable object.<html><head><title>The Array.from() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = Array.from("ABCDEFGH");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = a;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN8.jpg)Array.Keys() method: This method returns an Array Iterator object with the keys of an array as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The Array.keys() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Lotus", "Sunflower"];const keys = flowers.keys();let str="";for(let a of keys){str+=a+"<br>";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGN9.jpg)Array const: As we know that, it is a common practice to declare arrays using const. An array declared with const cannot be reassigned. If we try to do so, then the error is encountered as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Cannot Reassign a constant array</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>try {const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];flowers = ["Sunflower", "Lotus", "Kewda"];}catch (err) {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = err;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNA.jpg)Array elements can be reassigned as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Can Reassign a constant array</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];flowers[0]="Sunflower";flowers.push("Kewda");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNB.jpg)Note: const variables must be assigned a value when they are declared. Otherwise, the syntax error is encountered.Const block scope: An array declared with const has block scope.Example:<html><head><title>Declaring array with block scope</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];{const flowers = ["Sunflower", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers[0];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNC.jpg)An array declared with var does not have block scope.<html><head><title>Declaring array without block scope</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>var flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];{var flowers = ["Sunflower", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers[0];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGND.jpg)Redeclaring arrays: redeclaring an array declared with var is allowed anywhere in a program.Example:var flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];var flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];flowers=[“Sunflower”, Kewda];redeclaring an array to const, in the same scope, or in the same block, is not allowed.Example:var flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​//allowedconst flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowed{var flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​//allowedconst flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowed}Redeclaring an existing const array, in the same scope, or in the same block, is not allowed.Example:const flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// allowedconst flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowedvar flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowedflowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowed{const flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// allowedconst flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowedvar flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowedflowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowed}Redeclaring an array with const, in another scope, or in another block, is allowed.const flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// allowed{const flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// not allowed}{const flowers=[“Rose”, “Jasmin”, “Lilly”];​// allowed}CHAPTER 16DATESIntroduction: JavaScript uses the browser’s time zone by default and displays a date. The date object is created with the help of the new Date() constructor.There are four ways to create a new date object:-          new Date()-          new Date(year, month, day, hours, minutes, seconds, milliseconds)-          new Date(milliseconds)-          new Date(date string)new Date(): It is used to create a date object with the current date and time as shown in the following example:<html><head><title>The Date() constructor demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNE.jpg)new Date(year, month, day, hours, minutes, seconds, milliseconds): This creates a new date object with a specified date and time. 7 numbers specify year, month, day, hour, minute, second, and millisecond.Example:<html><head><title>The Date() constructor with 7 numbers demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date(2021, 08, 23, 20, 15, 0, 0);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNF.jpg)Note: JavaScript counts months from 0 to 11: January = 0 and December = 11\. In the above example, you can observe 08 represents September month.Specifying a month higher than 11, will not result in an error but add the overflow to the next year.Specifying a day higher than max, will not result in an error in an error but add the overflow to the next month.Six numbers specify year, month, day, hour, minute, second.Five numbers specify year, month, day, hour, minute.Four numbers specify year, month, day, hour.Three numbers specify year, month, day.Two numbers specify year, month.Note: we cannot omit a month. If we supply only one parameter it will be treated as milliseconds.Previous century: one and two digit years will be interpreted as 19xx.Example:<html><head><title>The new century demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date(99, 08, 23);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNG.jpg)new Date(dateString): It creates a new date object from a date string as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The date string demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date("September 23, 2021 20:32:00");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNH.jpg)new Date(milliseconds): It creates a new date object as zero time plus milliseconds. JavaScript stores dates as the number of milliseconds since January 01, 1970, 00:00:00 UTC (Universal Time Coordinated).Example:<html><head><title>The new Date(milliseconds) demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date(0);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNJ.jpg)Date Methods: There are numerous methods to set day, month, year and so on.The toDateString() method: This method is used to convert a date to a more readable format as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The toDateString() demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d.toDateString();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNK.jpg)The toISOString() method: This method converts a Date object to a string, using the ISO standard format.Example:<html><head><title>The toISOString() demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d.toISOString();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNM.jpg)JavaScript Date Input: There are three types of date formats:-          ISO Date-          Short Date-          Long DateJavaScript Date Output: JavaScript output dates in full text string format as shown below.Fri Sept 24 2021 20:40:00 GMT+0530 (India Standard Time)ISO Dates: ISO 8601 is the international standard for the representation of dates and times.Syntax: (YYYY-MM-DD)Example:<html><head><title>ISO Date Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript ISO Dates</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date("2021-09-24");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNN.jpg)ISO dates (year and month): Without day also, we can use ISO dates as (YYYY-MM).ISO dates (only year): Without month and day, we can use ISO dates as (YYYY).ISO dates (Date-Time): ISO dates with time can be written as (YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ).Example:<html><head><title>ISO Date with time Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript ISO Dates</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date("2021-09-24T20:52:00Z");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNP.jpg)Date and time are separated with a capital T and UTC time is defined with a capital Z.UTC (Universal Time Coordinated) is the same as GMT (Greenwich Mean Time).Omitting T or Z in a date-time string can give different results in different browsers.Time zones: When we do not specify the time zone, by default JavaScript will use the browser’s time zone. If a date/time is created in GMT, the date/time will be converted to CDT (Central US Daylight Time) if a user browsers from the central US.Short Dates: Short dates are written in “MM/DD/YYYY” format.Example:<html><head><title>Short Date Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript ISO Dates</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date("09/24/2021");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNR.jpg)Note: Some browsers may produce an error if months or days have no leading zeros. Some browsers will return NaN.Long dates: Long dates are normally written in a “MMM DD YYYY” format as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Long Date Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript ISO Dates</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date("sep 24 2021");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNS.jpg)Note: Month and day can be in any order and month can be written in full (September) or abbreviated (Sep).Parsing dates: if we have a valid date string, we can use the Date.parse() method to convert it to milliseconds between the date and January 1, 1970 as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Date.parse() method Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const millisecond = Date.parse("September 24, 2021");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = millisecond;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNT.jpg)We can also use the number of milliseconds to convert it to a date object as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Date.parse() method from millisecond to date object Demo</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const millisecond = Date.parse("September 24, 2021");const d=new Date(millisecond);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNU.jpg)Get date methods: These methods can be used for getting information from a date object.

| Method | Description |
| getFullYear() | Get the year as a four digit number (yyyy) |
| getMonth() | Get the month as a number (0-11) |
| getDate() | Get the day as a number (1-31) |
| getHours() | Get the hour (0-23) |
| getMinutes() | Get the minute (0-59) |
| getSeconds() | Get the second (0-59) |
| getMilliseconds() | Get the millisecond (0-999) |
| getTime() | Get the time (milliseconds since January 1, 1970) |
| getDay() | Get the weekday as a number (0-6) |
| Date.now() | Get the time. ECMAScript 5. |

The getTime() method: This method returns the number of milliseconds since January 1, 1970 as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The getTime() Method</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d.getTime();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNV.jpg)The getFullYear() method: This method is used to return the year of a date as a four digit number as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The getFullYear() Method</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d.getFullYear();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNW.jpg)The getMonth() method: This method is used to return the month of a date as a number from 0 to 11 as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The getMonth() Method</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d.getMonth();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNX.jpg)We can also use an array of names with this method to return the month as a name as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The getMonth() name Method</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();const months=["January","February","March","April","May","June","July","August","September","October","November","December"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = months[d.getMonth()];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNY.jpg)The getDate() method: This method is used to return the day of a date as a number from 1 to 31 as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The getDate() Method</title></head><body><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d.getDate();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGNZ.jpg)The getHours() method: This method is used to return the hours of a date as a number from 0 to 23.The getMinutes() method: This method is used to return the minutes of a date as a number from 0 to 59.The getSeconds() method: This method is used to return the seconds of a date as a number from 0 to 59.The getMilliseconds() method: This method is used to return the milliseconds of a date as a number from 0 to 999.An example that includes all of these methods is as shown below.<html><head><title>The getHour(), getMinutes(), getSeconds(), getMilliseconds Method</title></head><body><h4>The getHours() method</h4><p id="hours"></p><h4>The getMinutes() method</h4><p id="minutes"></p><h4>The getSeconds() method</h4><p id="seconds"></p><h4>The getMilliseconds() method</h4><p id="milliseconds"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("hours").innerHTML = d.getHours();document.getElementById("minutes").innerHTML = d.getMinutes();document.getElementById("seconds").innerHTML = d.getSeconds();document.getElementById("milliseconds").innerHTML = d.getMilliseconds();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP0.jpg)The getDay() method: This method is used to return the weekday of a date as a number from 0 to 6 as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The getDay() Method</title></head><body><h4>The getDay() method</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d.getDay();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP1.jpg)We can also use the getDay() method to print weekdays as a name as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The getDay() with name Method</title></head><body><h4>The getDay() with name method</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();const days = ["Sunday","Monday","Tuesday","Wednesday","Thursday","Friday","Saturday"];document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = days[d.getDay()];</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP2.jpg)Set date methods: These methods are used to set date values like year, months, days, hours, minutes, seconds, milliseconds.Set date methods are listed in the following table.

| Method | Description |
| setDate() | Set the day as a number (1-31) |
| setFullYear() | Set the year (optionally month and day) |
| setHours() | Set the hour (0-23) |
| setMilliseconds() | Set the milliseconds (0-999) |
| setMinutes() | Set the minutes (0-59) |
| setMonth() | Set the month (0-11) |
| setSeconds() | Set the seconds (0-59) |
| setTime() | Set the time (milliseconds since January 1, 1970) |

The setFullYear() method: This method is used to set the year of a date object as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The setFullYear() Method</title></head><body><h4>The setFullYear() Method</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();d.setFullYear(2021);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP3.jpg)This method is also used to set month and day as shown below.<html><head><title>The setFullYear() with month and day Method</title></head><body><h4>The setFullYear() Method</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();d.setFullYear(2021,08,25);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP4.jpg)The setMonth() method: This method is used to set the month of a date object from 0 to 11 as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The setMonth() Method</title></head><body><h4>The setMonth() Method</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();d.setMonth(8);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP5.jpg)The setDate() method: This method is used to set the day of a date object from 1 to 31 as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The setDate() Method</title></head><body><h4>The setDate() Method</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();d.setDate(25);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP6.jpg)This method is also used to add days to a date as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The setDate() Method with adding days</title></head><body><h4>The setDate() Method with adding days</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>const d = new Date();d.setDate(d.getDate()+25);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP7.jpg)The setHours() method: This method is used to set the hours of a date object from 0 to 23.The setMinutes() method: This method is used to set the minutes of a date object from 0 to 59.The setSeconds() method: This method is used to set the seconds of a date object from 0 to 59.An example to demonstrate all the above methods as below.<html><head><title>The setHour(), setMinutes(), setSeconds() Methods</title></head><body><h4>The setHours() method</h4><p id="hours"></p><h4>The setMinutes() method</h4><p id="minutes"></p><h4>The setSeconds() method</h4><p id="seconds"></p><script>const d = new Date();d.setHours(20);document.getElementById("hours").innerHTML = d;d.setMinutes(47);document.getElementById("minutes").innerHTML = d;d.setSeconds(15);document.getElementById("seconds").innerHTML = d;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP8.jpg)Compare dates: dates can be compared as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Comparing dates demo</title></head><body><h4>Comparing dates demo</h4><p id="sample"></p><script>let str;const today = new Date();const someday = new Date();someday.setFullYear(2021, 8, 24);if (someday > today) {str = "Today is before September 24, 2021.";} else {str = "Today is after September 24, 2021.";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGP9.jpg)CHAPTER 17MATHEMATICAL AND COMPARISON OPERATIONSIntroduction: In JavaScript Math objects are used to perform mathematical operations on numbers.Math property syntax:Math.propertyMath properties or constants provide 8 mathematical constants as shown in the following example.Math.E        // returns Euler's number
Math.PI       // returns PI
Math.SQRT2    // returns the square root of 2
Math.SQRT1_2  // returns the square root of 1/2
Math.LN2      // returns the natural logarithm of 2
Math.LN10     // returns the natural logarithm of 10
Math.LOG2E    // returns base 2 logarithm of E
Math.LOG10E   // returns base 10 logarithm of E<html><head><title>Math Constants Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Math Constants</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML ="<p><b>Math.E:</b> " + Math.E + "</p>" +"<p><b>Math.PI:</b> " + Math.PI + "</p>" +"<p><b>Math.SQRT2:</b> " + Math.SQRT2 + "</p>" +"<p><b>Math.SQRT1_2:</b> " + Math.SQRT1_2 + "</p>" +"<p><b>Math.LN2:</b> " + Math.LN2 + "</p>" +"<p><b>Math.LN10:</b> " + Math.LN10 + "</p>" +"<p><b>Math.LOG2E:</b> " + Math.LOG2E + "</p>" +"<p><b>Math.Log10E:</b> " + Math.LOG10E + "</p>";</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPA.jpg)Math methods: There are wide varieties of mathematical methods in JavaScript. Some of them are discussed in the following section.Syntax:Math.method(number);Number to integer methods: There are some methods in JavaScript that are used to round a number to an integer. The following are the list of methods that round off a number to an integer.

| Math.round(x) | Returns x rounded to its nearest integer |
|  |  |
| Math.ceil(x) | Returns x rounded up to its nearest integer |
|  |  |
| Math.floor(x) | Returns x rounded down to its nearest integer |
|  |  |
| Math.trunc(x) | Returns the integer part of x  |

Math.round() method: This method returns the nearest integer.ExampleMath.round(5.8);    // returns 6
Math.round(4.9);    // returns 5
Math.round(5.4);    // returns 5
Math.round(5.2);    // returns 5
Math.round(-3.1);    // returns -3Math.ceil() method: This method returns the value of the argument rounded up to its nearest integer.Example:Math.ceil(5.8);    // returns 6
Math.ceil(4.9);    // returns 5
Math.ceil(5.4);    // returns 6
Math.ceil(5.2);    // returns 6
Math.ceil(-3.1);    // returns -3Math.floor() method: This method is used to return the value of an argument rounded down to its nearest integer.Example:Math.floor(5.8);    // returns 5
Math.floor(4.9);    // returns 4
Math.floor(5.4);    // returns 5
Math.floor(5.2);    // returns 5
Math.floor(-3.1);    // returns -4Math.trunc() method: This method is used to return the integer part of the argument.Example:Math.floor(5.8);    // returns 5
Math.floor(4.9);    // returns 4
Math.floor(5.4);    // returns 5
Math.floor(5.2);    // returns 5
Math.floor(-3.1);    // returns -3Math.sign() method: This method is used to return negative if an argument is negative, positive if an argument is positive and null if an argument is not a number.Example:Math.sign(-5.8);    // returns -1
Math. sign (4.9);    // returns 1
Math. sign (0);    // returns 0
Math. sign (A);    // returns NULLMath.pow() method: This method returns the value of x to the power of y.Example:
Math.pow(2,5);​//returns 32Math.sqrt() method: This method returns the square root of an argument.Example:Math,sqrt(25);​​// returns 5Math.abs() method: This method returns the positive value of an argument.Example:Math.abs(-5.1)​// returns 5.1Math.sin() method: This method returns the sine value between -1 and 1 of the angle x given in radians.Note: If you want to change radians to degree, use the formula given below.Angle of radians=Angle in  degrees x PI/180.Example:Math.sin(90*Math.PI/180);​// returns 1Math.cos() method: This method returns the cosine value between -1 and 1 of the angle x given in radians.Note: If you want to change radians to degree, use the formula given below.Angle of radians=Angle in  degrees x PI/180.Example:Math.cos(0*Math.PI/180);​// returns 1Math.min() method: This method is used to find the lowest value in a list of arguments.Example:Math.min(12,10,3,-2,33,-5);​// returns -5Math.max() method: This method is used to find the highest value in a list of arguments.Example:Math.max(12,10,3,-2,33,-5);​// returns 33Math.random() method: This method returns a random number between 0 (inclusive) and 1 (exclusive).Math.random();​// returns a random numberMath.log() method: This method returns the natural logarithm of an argument.Example:Math.log(1)​​// returns 0Math.log2() method: This method returns the base 2 logarithm of an argument.Example:Math.log2(8);​// returns 3Math.log10() method: This method returns the base 10 logarithm of an argument.Example:Math.log10(1000);​​// returns 3All the above mathematical methods are covered in the following example.<html><head><title>The Math methods</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Math methods</h2><h5>The Math.round() method</h5><p id="round"></p><h5>The Math.ceil() method</h5><p id="ceil"></p><h5>The Math.floor() method</h5><p id="floor"></p><h5>The Math.trunc() method</h5><p id="trunc"></p><h5>The Math.sign() method</h5><p id="sign"></p><h5>The Math.pow() method</h5><p id="pow"></p><h5>The Math.sqrt() method</h5><p id="sqrt"></p><h5>The Math.abs() method</h5><p id="abs"></p><h5>The Math.sin() method</h5><p id="sin"></p><h5>The Math.cos() method</h5><p id="cos"></p><h5>The Math.min() method</h5><p id="min"></p><h5>The Math.max() method</h5><p id="max"></p><h5>The Math.random() method</h5><p id="random"></p><h5>The Math.log() method</h5><p id="log"></p><h5>The Math.log2() method</h5><p id="log2"></p><h5>The Math.log10() method</h5><p id="log10"></p><script>document.getElementById("round").innerHTML = Math.round(-3.1);document.getElementById("ceil").innerHTML = Math.ceil(4.2);document.getElementById("floor").innerHTML = Math.floor(3.6);document.getElementById("trunc").innerHTML = Math.trunc(10.5);document.getElementById("sign").innerHTML = Math.sign(-5.1);document.getElementById("pow").innerHTML = Math.pow(5,3);document.getElementById("sqrt").innerHTML = Math.sqrt(25);document.getElementById("abs").innerHTML = Math.abs(-6.8);document.getElementById("sin").innerHTML = Math.sin(90*Math.PI/180);document.getElementById("cos").innerHTML = Math.cos(0*Math.PI/180);document.getElementById("min").innerHTML = Math.min(-10,-3,5,33,4);document.getElementById("max").innerHTML = Math.max(-10,-3,5,33,4);document.getElementById("random").innerHTML = Math.random();document.getElementById("log").innerHTML = Math.log(1);document.getElementById("log2").innerHTML = Math.log2(8);document.getElementById("log10").innerHTML = Math.log10(1000);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPB.jpg)![](image_rsrcGPC.jpg)Math object methods are listed in the table below.

| Method | Description |
| [abs(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_abs.asp) | Returns the absolute value of x |
| [acos(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_acos.asp) | Returns the arccosine of x, in radians |
| [acosh(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_acosh.asp) | Returns the hyperbolic arccosine of x |
| [asin(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_asin.asp) | Returns the arcsine of x, in radians |
| [asinh(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_asinh.asp) | Returns the hyperbolic arcsine of x |
| [atan(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_atan.asp) | Returns the arctangent of x as a numeric value between -PI/2 and PI/2 radians |
| [atan2(y, x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_atan2.asp) | Returns the arctangent of the quotient of its arguments |
| [atanh(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_atanh.asp) | Returns the hyperbolic arctangent of x |
| [cbrt(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_cbrt.asp) | Returns the cubic root of x |
| [ceil(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_ceil.asp) | Returns x, rounded upwards to the nearest integer |
| [cos(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_cos.asp) | Returns the cosine of x (x is in radians) |
| [cosh(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_cosh.asp) | Returns the hyperbolic cosine of x |
| [exp(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_exp.asp) | Returns the value of Ex |
| [floor(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_floor.asp) | Returns x, rounded downwards to the nearest integer |
| [log(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_log.asp) | Returns the natural logarithm (base E) of x |
| [max(x, y, z, ..., n)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_max.asp) | Returns the number with the highest value |
| [min(x, y, z, ..., n)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_min.asp) | Returns the number with the lowest value |
| [pow(x, y)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_pow.asp) | Returns the value of x to the power of y |
| [random()](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_random.asp) | Returns a random number between 0 and 1 |
| [round(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_round.asp) | Rounds x to the nearest integer |
| [sign(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_sign.asp) | Returns if x is negative, null or positive (-1, 0, 1) |
| [sin(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_sin.asp) | Returns the sine of x (x is in radians) |
| [sinh(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_sinh.asp) | Returns the hyperbolic sine of x |
| [sqrt(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_sqrt.asp) | Returns the square root of x |
| [tan(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_tan.asp) | Returns the tangent of an angle |
| [tanh(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_tanh.asp) | Returns the hyperbolic tangent of a number |
| [trunc(x)](https://www.w3schools.com/jsref/jsref_trunc.asp) | Returns the integer part of a number (x) |

The Math.random() method: We have already covered the Math.random() method in the previous section. As we have come across that the random() method generates numbers between 0 (inclusive), and 1 (exclusive). It always returns a number less than 1.Random integers: Math.random() used with Math.floor() can be used to return random integers as shown in the following example.// Returns a random integer from 0 to 9:
Math.floor(Math.random() * 10);<html><head><title>Random integer demo</title></head><body><h2>Random integer demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =Math.floor(Math.random() * 10);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPD.jpg)// Returns a random integer from 0 to 10:
Math.floor(Math.random() * 11);// Returns a random integer from 0 to 99:
Math.floor(Math.random() * 100);Note: We can multiply any number with the random() method. The numbers will be generated randomly within that range.A proper random function: A proper random function always returns a random number between min (included) and max (excluded) as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>A proper random method demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Math.random()</h2><button onclick="document.getElementById('sample').innerHTML = getRndInteger(0,10)">Get Random Number!</button><p id="sample"></p><script>function getRndInteger(min, max) {  return Math.floor(Math.random() * (max - min)) + min;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPE.jpg)The code below returns a random number between min and max both included.<html><head><title>A proper random method included both min and max demo</title></head><body><h2>A proper random method included both min and max demo</h2><button onclick="document.getElementById('sample').innerHTML = getRndInteger(0,10)">Get Random Number!</button><p id="sample"></p><script>function getRndInteger(min, max) {  return Math.floor(Math.random() * (max - min+1)) + min;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPF.jpg)Boolean values: JavaScript supports Boolean values that result in either TRUE or FALSE. In some situations, we need a data type that returns one of the two values, like yes/no, on/off, TRUE/FALSE, high voltage/low voltage, bit 1/ bit 0\. In order to represent these types of data we use Boolean data type.The Boolean() function: This function is used to find out if an expression or a variable is true. Let’s have a look at the example below that returns true value.Boolean(6>5)​// returns true<html><head><title>The Boolean function demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Booleans</h2><h4>Display the value of Boolean(6>5)<h4><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = Boolean(6 > 5);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPG.jpg)Everything with a value greater than and less than zero is true. Value zero or without a value is false.Example: demonstrate the value greater than and less than zero is true<html><head><title>Booleans returns true</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Booleans</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML ="10 is " + Boolean(10) + "<br>" +"1.02 is " + Boolean(1.02) + "<br>" +"-5 is " + Boolean(-5) + "<br>" +"Any (not empty) string is " + Boolean("Hi") + "<br>" +"Even the string 'false' is " + Boolean('false') + "<br>" +"Any expression (except zero) is " + Boolean(2 + 3 + 1.02);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPH.jpg)Example: demonstrate the value zero or without a value is false.<html><head><title>Booleans returns false</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Booleans</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let a,b=null,c=false;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML ="0 is " + Boolean(0) + "<br>" +"-0 is " + Boolean(-0) + "<br>" +"empty string is " + Boolean("") + "<br>" +"undefined is " + Boolean(a) + "<br>" +"Null is false is " + Boolean(b) + "<br>" +"Boolean value false is " + Boolean(false) + "<br>" +"The Boolean value NaN is "+Boolean(5/"hi");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPJ.jpg)Booleans can be objects: Booleans are primitive values created from literals.Examplelet a=false;this same statement can also be written as,let b=new Boolean(false);Now, we have created an object with the keyword new.Note: Boolean objects slow down execution speed. The new keyword can produce an unexpected result.Example:<html><head><title>Boolean is compared with Boolean object</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Booleans</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = false;         // a is a booleanlet b = new Boolean(false);  // b is an objectdocument.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = (a==b);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPK.jpg)When using the === operator, equal Booleans are not equal, because this operator expects equality in both type and value as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Boolean is compared with Boolean object using === operator</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Booleans</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = false;         // a is a booleanlet b = new Boolean(false);  // b is an objectdocument.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = (a===b);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPM.jpg)Note: Objects cannot be compared. The difference between (a==b) and (a===b) and comparing two objects will always return false values.Comparison operators: comparison and logical operators return either true or false value. Comparison operators are used logical statements or selection statements to determine equality or difference between variables or values.Let us consider, a=6\. The following table shows all comparison operators.

| Operator | Description |
| == | Equal to |
| === | Equal value and equal type |
| != | Not equal to |
| !== | Not equal value or not equal type |
| > | Greater than |
| < | Less than |
| >= | Greater than or equal to |
| <= | Less than or equal to |

Logical operators: Logical operators are used to return Boolean values i.e., either true or false values. These logical operators can be used with comparison operators.Let us consider, a=2 and b=3, the logical operators are explained in the table below.

| Operator | Description |
| && | Logical AND operator |
| &#124;&#124; | Logical OR operator |
| ! | Logical NOT operator |

Conditional or ternary operator: The operators that operate on three or more operands are called conditional operators.Syntax:variablename=(condition) ? value1 : value2;Example:<html><head><title>Ternary operator demo</title></head><body><h2>Ternary operator demo</h2><p>Input a number and click the button:</p><input id="even" value="6" /><button onclick="ternaryFunction()">Check it!</button><p id="sample"></p><script>function ternaryFunction() {  let n = document.getElementById("even").value;  let even = (n%2==0) ? "Even Number":"Odd Number";  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = even;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPN.jpg)Clicking on Check it! Button we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGPP.jpg)We can also change the value in the text box and click on Check it! Button and see the result shown below.![](image_rsrcGPR.jpg)If the value of the variable is even then it prints an even number otherwise, an odd number.Comparing different types: When comparing a string with a number, JavaScript will convert the string to a number. An empty string converts to zero. A non-numeric string converts to NaN which is always false.The following table shows some of the true and false value returning comparisons.

| Example | Result |
| 5<6 | True |
| 5<”6” | True |
| 5<”Sneha” | False |
| 5>”Swaroop” | False |
| 5==”Sonia” | False |
| “5”<”6” | True |
| “5”==”6” | False |

Note: Variables should be converted to the proper type before comparison.Example:<html><head><title>Ternary operator proper type comparison demo</title></head><body><h2>Ternary operator demo</h2><p>Input a number and click the button:</p><input id="even" value="6" /><button onclick="ternaryFunction()">Check it!</button><p id="sample"></p><script>function ternaryFunction() {  let even;  let n = Number(document.getElementById("even").value);  if(isNaN(n)){  even="Input is not a number";  }else  {  even = (n%2==0) ? "Even Number":"Odd Number";  }  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = even;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPS.jpg)If we enter other than numbers, we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGPT.jpg)CHAPTER 18SELECTION AND ITERATIVE STATEMENTSIntroduction: In general instructions written are executed in a linear fashion called sequential order. It is not mandatory that all the instructions are to be executed in the order in which they are written, in some situations, the control may bifurcate based on the condition, some statements get executed repeatedly a certain number of times based on the condition. Some statements may jump from one statement to another based on the condition imposed.JavaScript supports both selection statements and iterative statements.selection statements: selection statements are also called conditional control statements used to check the condition, based on the condition, the control makes decision and branching.The selection statements are classified as,-          if-statement-          if-else statement-          if-else-if statement-          switch statementif statement: if statement is also called a one-way branching statement. In simple if statements, if the condition is true then the statement or set of statements will get executed. If the condition is false then the next statements, if any, will get executed.The general form of if statement is,if(condition)​​OR​​if(condition)statement1;​​{​statement1;​statement2;​…​statement-n​}Example:<html><head><title>if statement demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript if</h2><p id="sample"><script>let a=10,b=20,large=a;if (b > large){large=b;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Largest Number is " + large;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPU.jpg)if-else statement: if-else statement is also called two-way branching. In an if-else statement the condition will be checked, if the condition is true then statement 1 will get executed. Otherwise, else part i.e. statement 2 will get executed.The general form of if-else statement is,if(condition)​statement 1;elsestatement 2;Example: Code to find largest of two numbers using if-else statement<html><head><title>if-else statement demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript if-else</h2><p id="sample"><script>let a=10,b=20;if (a > b){document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Largest Number is " + a;}else{document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Largest Number is " + b;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPV.jpg)if-else-if statement: this statement is also called an if-else-if ladder. There are several alternatives available, out of which we have to choose the statement that matches with the condition. It is also called multiway branching.The general form of if-else-if statement is,if(condition1)​statement1;else​if(condition2)​statement2;​else​----​elseif(condition-n)​statement-n;​else​default statement;First condition 1 is tested. If the condition is true then statement 1 is executed. Otherwise, condition 2 is tested. If condition 2 is true then statement 2 is executed. Otherwise, condition 3 is tested and so on. Finally, condition-n is tested. If it is true, statement-n is executed. If none of the conditions is true then the default statement is executed.Example: The following code declares the class depending on percentage.<html><head><title>if-else-if demo</title></head><body><h2>if-else-if demo</h2><p>Input a percentage and click the button:</p><input id="percentage" value="" /><button onclick="perFunction()">Declare Class</button><p id="distinction"></p><p id="firstclass"></p><p id="secondclass"></p><p id="passclass"></p><p id="fail"></p><script>function perFunction() {let per = document.getElementById("percentage").value;if(per>=85&&per<=100)document.getElementById("distinction").innerHTML = "Distinction";elseif(per>=60&&per<85)document.getElementById("firstclass").innerHTML = "First Class";elseif(per>=50&&per<60)document.getElementById("secondclass").innerHTML = "Second Class";elseif(per>=35&&per<50)document.getElementById("passclass").innerHTML = "Pass Class";elsedocument.getElementById("fail").innerHTML = "Fail";}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPW.jpg)switch statement: it is an alternative of if-else-if ladder. It is used to select one block of code if the expression present in the switch statement matches with the case label. Here switch expression will hold only one of the three values, int, char or Boolean values. This statement provides multiple way branching.The general form of switch statement is,switch(expression){​case label-1: statement 1;​break;​case label-2: statement 2;​break;​…​case label-n: statement n;​break;​default: default-statement;}The break statement is used to indicate that the case label is terminated. The default statement will get executed if the switch expression does not match with any case label.Example: The following code displays the current weekday.<html><head><title>switch statement demo</title></head><body><h2>switch statement demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let day;switch (new Date().getDay()) {  case 0:    day = "Sunday";    break;  case 1:    day = "Monday";    break;  case 2:    day = "Tuesday";    break;  case 3:    day = "Wednesday";    break;  case 4:    day = "Thursday";    break;  case 5:    day = "Friday";    break;  case  6:    day = "Saturday";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "Today is " + day;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPX.jpg)Iterative statements: iterative statements that allow repeated execution of a set of instructions till a certain condition is satisfied. Iterative statements are also called looping statements.Types of loops:-          for loop – loops through a block of code a number of times.-          for/in – loops through the properties of an object.-          for/of – loops through the values of an iterable object.-          while – loops through a block of code while a specified condition is true.-          do/while – loops through a block of code while a specified condition is true.for loop: this looping statement is used when there are a fixed number of iterations. When we know in advance exactly how many times a set of statements should be repeatedly executed.The general form of for loop is,for(initialization; condition; increment/decrement){Statement1;Statement2;…Statement-n;}Example: The following code to display natural numbers.<html><head><title>for loop demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript For Loop</h2><p>Natural Numbers</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let str="";for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPY.jpg)Loop scope: Using var in for loop gives the following result.<html><head><title>Using var in for loop</title></head><body><h2>Using var in for loop</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>var i = 5;for (var i = 0; i < 10; i++) {  // some statements}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = i;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGPZ.jpg)Note: Using var the variable declared in the loop redeclares the variable outside the loop.Using let in for loop gives the following result.<html><head><title>Using let in for loop</title></head><body><h2>Using let in for loop</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let i = 5;for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) {  // some statements}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = i;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR0.jpg)Note: Using let, the variable declared in the loop does not redeclare the variable outside the loop. When let is used to declare the i variable in a loop, the i variable will be visible within the loop.The for in loop: This statement loops through the properties of an object.Syntax:for (key in object) {
  // code block to be executed
}Example:<html><head><title>for in loop demo</title></head><body><h2>For In Loop</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const family_member = {fname:"Hanumanth", lname:"Ladwa", age:38};let str = "";for (let a in family_member) {  str += family_member[a] + " ";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR1.jpg)for in over arrays: for in statement can also loop over properties of an array.Syntax:for (variable in array) {
  code
}Example:<html><head><title>for in loop over arrays</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript For In</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [10, 20, 30, 40, 50];let str = "";for (let p in a) {str += a[p] + "<br>";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR2.jpg)Note: It is better to use a for loop, a for of loop, or Array.forEach() when the index order is important.Array.forEach() method: This method calls a function once for each array element.Example:<html><head><title>Array.forEach() demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Array.forEach()</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const a = [10, 20, 30, 40, 50];let str = "";a.forEach(arrayFunction);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;function arrayFunction(value, index, array) {str += value + "<br>";}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR3.jpg)The for of loop: This looping statement loops through the values of an iterable object.Syntax:for (variable of iterable) {
  // code block to be executed
}Here, a variable is used for every iteration the value of the next property is assigned to the variable. Variables can be declared with const, let or var. iterable means, an object that has iterable properties.Looping over an array:Example:<html><head><title>for of loop demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript For Of Loop</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly"];let str = "";for (let a of flowers) {  str += a + "<br>";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR4.jpg)while loop: it is also called a pretested looping structure, because the condition is checked before repeated execution of a set of statements.General form,while(condition)​​OR​​while(condition)statement;​​{​statement 1;​statement 2;​…​statement n;​}Example: JavaScript code that prints the first 5 even numbers using a while loop.<html><head><title>while loop demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript For Loop</h2><p>Even Numbers</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let str="";let i=2;while (i <= 10) {if(i%2==0)document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";i=i+2;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR5.jpg)do-while loop: in this looping structure the condition will be checked executing a set of instructions at least once, hence it is also called a post-tested looping structure.General form of do-while loop is,do​​do{​​{Statement1;​​OR​​statement 1;}while(condition);​​statement 2;​…​Statement n;​}while(condition);Example: JavaScript code to find the sum of first 5 odd numbers.<html><head><title>do while loop demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript do while Loop</h2><p>Sum of first five odd Numbers</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let i=1,sum=0;while (i <= 10) {if(i%2!=0)document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = sum+=i;i=i+2;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR6.jpg)break statement: break statement can be used in two ways; one we have already used in switch statements to transfer the control out of switch statements. Another is used to terminate the loop.General form of break statement is,break;Example: JavaScript code that breaks out of the for loop using a break statement.<html><head><title>break statement demo</title></head><body><h2>break statement demo</h2><p>Natural Numbers</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let str="";for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {if(i==3)break;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR7.jpg)Here, the break statement ends the loop when the loop index i is 3.continue statement: continue statement is used to skip the part of a looping statement and does not terminate the loop.The general form of continue statement is,continue;Example:<html><head><title>continue statement demo</title></head><body><h2>continue statement demo</h2><p>Natural Numbers</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let str="";for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {if(i==3)continue;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str+=i+"<br>";}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR8.jpg)This example skips the value of 3.Labels: labels and colon are preceded by the statements.Syntax:label:statements;The break and continue statements are the statements that can jump out of a block.Syntax:break labelname;continue label name;The continue statement with or without a label reference can only be used to skip one loop iteration.The break statement, without a label reference, can be used to jump out of a loop or a switch statement. With a label reference, the break statement can be used to jump out of any code block.Example:<html><head><title>break label demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript break label</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = ["Rose", "Jasmin", "Lilly", "Sunflower"];let str = "";list: {str += flowers[0] + "<br>";str += flowers[1] + "<br>";  break list;str += flowers[2] + "<br>";str += flowers[3] + "<br>";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGR9.jpg)Sets: A JavaScript set is a collection of unique values and each value can have its occurrence only at once.Some of the set methods are listed in the following table.

| Method | Description |
| new Set() | Creates a new Set |
| add() | Adds a new element to the Set |
| delete() | Removes an element from a Set |
| has() | Returns true if a value exists in the Set |
| forEach() | Invokes a callback for each element in the Set |
| values() | Returns an iterator with all the values in a Set |
| Property | Description |
| size | Returns the number elements in a Set |

Creating a set: A set can be created by using the new Set() method. And use the add() method to insert values and variables into the set.Example:<html><head><title>Set Demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Sets</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const alphabets = new Set(["a","b","c","e","f"]);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = alphabets.size;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRA.jpg)Creating set and adding values<html><head><title>Adding Set values Demo</title></head><body><h2>Adding Set values Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const alphabets = new Set();alphabets.add("a");alphabets.add("b");alphabets.add("c");alphabets.add("d");alphabets.add("e");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = alphabets.size;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRB.jpg)Creating a set and adding variables.<html><head><title>Adding variables to a Set Demo</title></head><body><h2>Adding variables to a Set Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const alphabets = new Set();const a="a";const b="b";const c="c";const d="d";const e="e";alphabets.add(a);alphabets.add(b);alphabets.add(c);alphabets.add(d);alphabets.add(e);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = alphabets.size;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRC.jpg)The add() method: This method is used to add the new elements into the existing set as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Adding elements to a Set Demo</title></head><body><h2>Adding elements to a Set Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const alphabets = new Set(["a","b","c","d","e"]);alphabets.add("f");alphabets.add("g");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = alphabets.size;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRD.jpg)Note: If you add equal elements, only the first will be saved.The forEach() method: This method calls a function for each set element.Example:<html><head><title>forEach() Set Demo</title></head><body><h2>forEach() Set Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const alphabets = new Set(["a","b","c","d","e"]);let str="";alphabets.forEach(function(value){str+=value;})document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRE.jpg)The values() method: This method returns a new iterator object containing all the values in a set.<html><head><title>set values() method Demo</title></head><body><h2>set values() method Demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const alphabets = new Set(["a","b","c","d","e"]);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = alphabets.values();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRF.jpg)Now, we can use the iterator object to access the elements as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>iterator object to access elements</title></head><body><h2>iterator object to access elements</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const alphabets = new Set(["a","b","c","d","e"]);let str="";for(const a of alphabets.values()){str+=a;}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRG.jpg)Maps: maps can be any data type that holds key values pairs and it remembers the original insertion order of the keys.Some of the map methods are as listed in the following table.

| Method | Description |
| new Map() | Creates a new Map |
| set() | Sets the value for a key in a Map |
| get() | Gets the value for a key in a Map |
| delete() | Removes a Map element specified by the key |
| has() | Returns true if a key exists in a Map |
| forEach() | Calls a function for each key/value pair in a Map |
| entries() | Returns an iterator with the [key, value] pairs in a Map |
| Property | Description |
| size | Returns the number of elements in Map |

Map can be created by passing an array to the new Map() method or creating a Map and using Map set() method as shown below.The new Map() method: We can create a Map by passing an Array to the new
