Map() constructor as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Map Objects</h2><p>Creating a Map from an Array:</p><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([  ["Rose", 60],  ["Jasmin", 20],  ["Lilly", 50]]);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.get("Rose");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRH.jpg)The set() method: This method is used to add elements to a Map as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using set() method</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Map Objects using set() method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map();flowers.set("Rose", 60);flowers.set("Jasmin", 20);flowers.set("Lilly", 50);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.get("Rose");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRJ.jpg)The set() method can also be used to change the Map values as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using set() method to change values</title></head><body><h2>Map objects using set() method to change values</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([["Rose", 60],["Jasmin", 20],["Lilly", 50]]);flowers.set("Rose",50);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.get("Rose");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRK.jpg)The get() method: This method is used to get the value of a key in a Map as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using get() method</title></head><body><h2>Map objects using get() method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([["Rose", 60],["Jasmin", 20],["Lilly", 50]]);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.get("Rose");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRM.jpg)The size property: This property returns the number of elements in a Map as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using size property</title></head><body><h2>Map objects using size property</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([["Rose", 60],["Jasmin", 20],["Lilly", 50]]);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.size;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRN.jpg)The delete() method: This method is used to remove elements from a Map as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using delete() method</title></head><body><h2>Map objects using delete() method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([["Rose", 60],["Jasmin", 20],["Lilly", 50]]);flowers.delete("Rose");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.size;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRP.jpg)The has() method: This method is used to return true if a key exists in a Map otherwise, false as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using has() method</title></head><body><h2>Map objects using has() method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([["Rose", 60],["Jasmin", 20],["Lilly", 50]]);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = flowers.has("Rose");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRR.jpg)The forEach() method: This method calls a function for each key or value pair in a Map as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using forEach() method</title></head><body><h2>Map objects using forEach() method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([["Rose", 60],["Jasmin", 20],["Lilly", 50]]);let str="";flowers.forEach(function(value,key){str+=key+" = " +value+ "<br>"})document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRS.jpg)The entries() method: This method returns an iterator object with the [key,values] in a Map as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Map objects using entries() method</title></head><body><h2>Map objects using entries() method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const flowers = new Map([["Rose", 60],["Jasmin", 20],["Lilly", 50]]);let str="";for(const a of flowers.entries()){str += a + "<br>";}document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = str;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRT.jpg)type conversion: it is a process of converting one type of data into another type. The conversion takes place by using the JavaScript function or automatically by JavaScript itself. JavaScript supports the following conversions.-          Converting Strings to Number-          Converting Number to Strings-          Converting Dates to Number-          Converting Numbers to Dates-          Converting Booleans to Numbers-          Converting Numbers to BooleansConverting Strings to Numbers: The method Number() can convert strings to numbers. Example, strings containing numbers like “1.25” convert to numbers like 1.25 and empty strings convert to 0 and anything else converts to NaN i.e., Not a Number.Number methods: The following table lists the number methods.

| Method | Description |
| Number() | Returns a number, converted from its argument |
|  |  |
| parseFloat() | Parses a string and returns a floating point number |
| parseInt() | Parses a string and returns an integer |

The unary + operator: Can be used to convert a variable to a number as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>typeof operator using + operator demo</title></head><body><h2>The JavaScript typeof Operator</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = "10";let b = + a;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = typeof a + "<br>" + typeof b;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRU.jpg)Note: if the variable cannot be converted, it will still be a number, but with the value NaN.Converting numbers to strings: The String() method is used to convert numbers to strings as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>String() method demo</title></head><body><h2>The JavaScript String() Method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let a = 456;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =  String(a) + "<br>" +  String(456) + "<br>" +  String(450 + 6);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRV.jpg)Note: The toString() method does the same.Some more methods are listed in the table below.

| Method | Description |
| toExponential() | Returns a string, with a number rounded and written using exponential notation. |
| toFixed() | Returns a string, with a number rounded and written with a specified number of decimals. |
| toPrecision() | Returns a string, with a number written with a specified length |

Converting dates to numbers: The Number() method is used to convert dates to numbers as shown in the following example.d = new Date();
Number(d)          // returns 1404568027739The date method getTime() does the same.Converting dates to strings: The String() method and toString() method can convert dates to strings.Example.String(Date())Date().toString()Some of the methods that are used to convert dates to strings are listed in the table below.

| Method | Description |
| getDate() | Get the day as a number (1-31) |
| getDay() | Get the weekday a number (0-6) |
| getFullYear() | Get the four digit year (yyyy) |
| getHours() | Get the hour (0-23) |
| getMilliseconds() | Get the milliseconds (0-999) |
| getMinutes() | Get the minutes (0-59) |
| getMonth() | Get the month (0-11) |
| getSeconds() | Get the seconds (0-59) |
| getTime() | Get the time (milliseconds since January 1, 1970) |

Converting Booleans to numbers: The number() method can also be used to convert Booleans to numbers.Example:Number(false)​// returns 0Number(true)​// returns 1Converting Booleans to strings: The String() and toString() methods are used to convert Booleans to strings.String(false)​// returns “false”String(true)​// returns “true”false.toString()​// returns “false”true.toString()​// returns “true”Automatic type conversion: JavaScript tries to convert to a suitable type automatically as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>Automatic Type Conversion demo</title></head><body><h2>Automatic Type Conversion demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =(10 + null) + "<br>"  +("10" + null) + "<br>" +("10" + 5) + "<br>" +("10" - 5) + "<br>" +("10" * "5") + "<br>" +("10" / "5") + "<br>"</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRW.jpg)Regular expression: It is a sequence of characters that forms a search pattern. When we search for data in a text, we can use this search pattern to describe what you are searching for. Regular expressions can be used to perform text search and text replace operations.Syntax:/pattern/modifiers;Example:/Hanumanth/i;Where, Hanumanth is a pattern and ‘i’ is a case insensitive modifier.The search() and replace() methods are used with regular expression.The search() method: This method is used to search a string for a specified value and returns the position of the match.Example:<html><head><title>The search() method demo</title></head><body><h2>The search() method demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = "Hanumanth Ladwa";let n = str.search("Ladwa");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = n;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRX.jpg)Note: If we want to use a regular expression with case insensitive search, we use /ladwa/iThe replace() method: This method replaces a specified value with another value in a string as shown in the following example.<html><head><title>The replace() method demo</title></head><body><h2>The replace() method demo</h2><p id="sample">Kindly read my Book</p><script>let str = document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML=str.replace("Book", "Bio");</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRY.jpg)Note: If we want to use a regular expression with case insensitive replace() method, we use /book/iRegular expression modifiers: These are listed in the table below.

| Modifier | Description |
| i | Perform case-insensitive matching |
| g | Perform a global match (find all matches rather than stopping after the first match) |
| m | Perform multiline matching |

Regular expression patterns: brackets are used to find a range of characters.

| Expression | Description |
| [abc] | Find any of the characters between the brackets |
| [0-9] | Find any of the digits between the brackets |
| (x&#124;y) | Find any of the alternatives separated with &#124; |

Metacharacters are characters with a special meaning as shown in the following table.

| Metacharacter | Description |
| \d | Find a digit |
| \s | Find a whitespace character |
| \b | Find a match at the beginning of a word like this: \bWORD, or at the end of a word like this: WORD\b |
| \uxxxx | Find the Unicode character specified by the hexadecimal number xxxx |

Quantifiers are used to define quantities as given in the table below.

| Quantifier | Description |
| n+ | Matches any string that contains at least one n |
| n* | Matches any string that contains zero or more occurrences of n |
| n? | Matches any string that contains zero or one occurrences of n |

The RegExp object: It is a regular expression object with predefined properties and methods.The test() method: This method is used to search a string for a pattern and returns either true or false value, depending on the result. Let’s look at the example.<html><head><title>The test() method</title></head><body><h2>The test() method</h2><p id="sample1">The sun rises in the east</p><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = document.getElementById("sample1").innerHTML;const pattern = /u/;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = pattern.test(str);</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGRZ.jpg)The exec() method: This method searches a string for a specified pattern, and returns the found text as an object. If no match is found, it returns a null. Let’s have an example.<html><head><title>The exec() method</title></head><body><h2>The exec() method</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>const obj = /e/.exec("The sun rises in the east");document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML ="Found " + obj[0] + " in position " + obj.index + " in the text: " + obj.input;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS0.jpg)CHAPTER 19EXCEPTION HANDLINGIntroduction: it is very common to make mistakes while writing any computer programs. Such mistakes made by the programmer are called errors. If there are errors present in the program, the program may produce an unintended result. Sometimes programs terminate abruptly. It is always true that, whenever we throw a garbage, we get garbage i.e., garbage in and garbage out (GIGO). If the input is not proper then the output is also not proper.In order to handle errors or mistakes in a JavaScript program we use error handling keywords as given below.-          try – this statement is used to monitor the statement or set of statements for errors.-          catch – this statement is used to handle the error.- throw – this statement is used to create custom errors, which creates an error object and throws it whenever error occurs.- finally – this statement is used to execute a statement or set of statements even though there are errors in the code. The finally statement will definitely execute.Errors may be generated due to mistakes while coding, misspelled statements by the programmer or not following the syntax of the programming language.In the example below, we intentionally typed allert instead of alert, it is misspelled. This type of mistakes can be handled by the try block.<html><head><title>The Error handling</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Error Handling</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>try {  allert("Hi there!");}catch(e) {  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.message;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS1.jpg)Note: JavaScript catches allert as an error and catch block will handle it.The try and catch block: The try statement is used to test for errors while it is being executed. The catch statement is used to execute a block of code if there is an error in the try block.Syntax:try{set of statements to be monitored for errors;}catch(e){Set of statements that handle error;}Throws errors: It is a process in which the custom error object is created and thrown. JavaScript exceptions could be String, a Number, a Boolean or an Object.Example:throw “Text message”;​// this throws a text exceptionthrow 0;​// this throws a Boolean exceptionExample to demonstrate custom exceptions that validates input.<html><head><title>Custom Exception</title></head><body><p>Please input marks between 0 and 100:</p><input id="sample" type="text"><button type="button" onclick="customFunction()">Check Marks</button><p id="sample1"></p><script>function customFunction() {  const msg = document.getElementById("sample1");  msg.innerHTML = "";  let a = document.getElementById("sample").value;  try {    if(a == "") throw "empty";    if(isNaN(a)) throw "not a number";    a = Number(a);    if(a < 0) throw "Negative marking not allowed";    if(a > 100) throw "Marks crossing 100";  }  catch(e) {    msg.innerHTML = "Input is " + e;  }}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS2.jpg)![](image_rsrcGS3.jpg)The finally statement: This statement will definitely execute even though there is an error in the try block.Syntax:try{set of statements to be monitored for errors;}catch(e){Set of statements that handle error;}finally{Set of statements that are to be executed regardless of the try and catch block;}Example:<html><head><title>The finally block demo</title></head><body><p>Please input marks between 0 and 100:</p><input id="sample" type="text"><button type="button" onclick="customFunction()">Check Marks</button><p id="sample1"></p><script>function customFunction() {  const msg = document.getElementById("sample1");  msg.innerHTML = "";  let a = document.getElementById("sample").value;  try {    if(a == "") throw "empty";    if(isNaN(a)) throw "not a number";    a = Number(a);    if(a < 0) throw "Negative marking not allowed";    if(a > 100) throw "Marks crossing 100";  }  catch(e) {    msg.innerHTML = "Input is " + e;  }finally{document.getElementById("sample").value="";}}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS4.jpg)Upon clicking on the Check Marks button, we get the following result.![](image_rsrcGS5.jpg)The error object: When error occurs, JavaScript throws a built in error object. This object has two properties namely, name and message.The following table lists the error name values.

| Error Name | Description |
| EvalError | An error has occurred in the eval() function |
| RangeError | A number "out of range" has occurred |
| ReferenceError | An illegal reference has occurred |
| SyntaxError | A syntax error has occurred |
| TypeError | A type error has occurred |
| URIError | An error in encodeURI() has occurred |

The EvalError: This error indicates an error in the eval() function.The RangeError: This error is thrown when a number lies outside the range of values.Example:<html><head><title>The RangeError demo</title></head><body><h2>The RangeError demo</h2><p id="sample"><script>let n = 1;try {  n.toPrecision(500);}catch(e) {  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.name;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS6.jpg)The ReferenceError: This error is thrown if we use reference i.e., a variable that has not been declared.<html><head><title>The ReferenceError demo</title></head><body><h2>The ReferenceError demo</h2><p id="sample"><script>let n = 1;try {n=a+1;}catch(e) {  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.name;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS7.jpg)The SyntaxError: This error is thrown if we try to evaluate code with a syntax error.Example:<html><head><title>The SyntaxError demo</title></head><body><h2>The SyntaxError demo</h2><p id="sample"><script>try {eval(String('Hanumanth Ladwa'));}catch(e) {  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.name;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS8.jpg)Type error: This error is thrown if we use a value that is out of the range of expected types.Example:<html><head><title>The TypeError demo</title></head><body><h2>The TypeError demo</h2><p id="sample"><script>let n=1;try {n.toLowerCase();}catch(e) {  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.name;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGS9.jpg)The URIError: This is thrown if we use illegal characters in a URI function.Example:<html><head><title>The URIError demo</title></head><body><h2>The URIError demo</h2><p id="sample"><script>try {decodeURI("%");}catch(e) {  document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = e.name;}</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGSA.jpg)CHAPTER 20CLASSESIntroduction: JavaScript classes are templates for JavaScript objects. Use the keyword class to create a class and always add a method called constructor() as shown in the example below.class class_name{constructor(){…}}Example:class add{
constructor(a,b){this.a=a;this.b=b;}}Note: if the variables declared and the parameter names are the same, we use this keyword in order to distinguish between the two.Example code to demonstrate the use of class.<html><head><title>The class demo</title></head><body><h2>JavaScript Class</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>class add {  constructor(a, b) {    this.a = a;    this.b = b;  }}const sum = new add(10, 20);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =sum.a + sum.b;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGSB.jpg)Constructor: A constructor method is used to initialize object properties. It has the same name as that of the class name. constructors execute automatically as soon as the object is created.Class methods: When creating a class add a constructor method then add any number of methods as shown in the following syntax:class class_name{constructor(){ … }method1() { … }method2() { … }method3() { … }}Example:<html><head><title>The class method demo</title></head><body><h2>The class method demo</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>class add {  constructor(a, b) {    this.a = a;    this.b = b;  }sum(){return sum.a + sum.b;}}const sum = new add(10, 20);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =sum.sum();</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGSC.jpg)JSON: JSON stands for JavaScript Object Notation. This is a format for storing and transporting data. It is often used when data is sent from a server to a webpage. It is a lightweight data interchange format. It is language independent.Example:{
"family_members":[
  {"firstName":"Priyanka", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Sneha", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Swaroop", "lastName":"Niranjan"}
]
}Note that, in JSON code, a name/value pair consists of a property name in double quotations, followed by a colon, followed by a value. JSON objects are written inside curly braces. JSON arrays are written in square brackets.Converting a JSON text to a JavaScript object: To convert JSON text to a JavaScript object we use JSON.parse() method as shown in the following example.let str=’{
"family_members":[
  {"firstName":"Priyanka", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Sneha", "lastName":"Niranjan"},
  {"firstName":"Swaroop", "lastName":"Niranjan"}
]
}’;const obj=JSON.parse(str);Example:<html><head><title>JSON demo</title></head><body><h2>Create Object from JSON String</h2><p id="sample"></p><script>let str = '{"family_members":[' +'{"firstName":"Priyanka","lastName":"Niranjan" },' +'{"firstName":"Sneha","lastName":"Niranjan" },' +'{"firstName":"Swaroop","lastName":"Niranjan" }]}';const obj = JSON.parse(str);document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML =obj.family_members[1].firstName + " " + obj.family_members[1].lastName;</script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGSD.jpg)JavaScript Reserved Words

JavaScript code to display all 12 months of a specified year.<html><head><script language="javascript">function day_title(day_name){document.write("<td align=center width=35 bgcolor=lightblue><b>"+day_name+"</b></td>");}function fill_table(month,month_len){day=1;document.write("<table border=1 cellspacing=3 cellpadding=3%>");document.write("<td colspan=7 align=center bgcolor=red><b>"+month+" "+year+"</b><tr>");day_title("Sun");day_title("Mon");day_title("Tue");day_title("Wed");day_title("Thu");day_title("Fri");day_title("Sat");document.write("</tr><tr>");for(var i=1; i<start_day;i++){document.write("<td>");}for(var i=start_day; i<8;i++){document.write("<td align=center bgcolor=silver>"+day+"</td>");day++;}document.write("<tr>");while(day<=month_len){for(var i=1; i<=7 && day<=month_len;i++){document.write("<td align=center bgcolor=silver>"+day+"</td>");day++;}document.write("<tr>");start_day=i;}document.write("</tr></table><br>");}year=prompt("enter 4 digit year ",2016);today=new Date("January 1, "+year);start_day=today.getDay()+1;fill_table("January", 31);if (year%4==0)fill_table("February", 29);elsefill_table("February", 28);fill_table("March", 31);fill_table("April", 30);fill_table("May", 31);fill_table("June", 30);fill_table("July", 31);fill_table("August", 31);fill_table("September", 30);fill_table("October", 31);fill_table("November", 30);fill_table("December", 31);</script></head></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGSK.jpg)Once we type 2021 and click on the OK button, we get the calendar from January 2021 to December 2021 as shown below.![](image_rsrcGSM.jpg)/* 7\. Your Score is: "+i);}</script></head><body bgcolor=silver><form onSubmit="exam(this)"><center><h1><blink>WELCOME TO ONLINE EXAM FORM</blink></h1></center><p><h1>1)Which is platform independent language</h1><input type="radio" name="one" value="c++"><label>c++</label><input type="radio" name="one" value="c"><label>c</label><input type="radio" name="one" value="java"><label>java</label><input type="radio" name="one"value="basic"><label>BASIC</label></p><p><h1>2) Which is class of all class in java</h1><input type="radio" name="three" value="object"><label>Object</label><input type="radio" name="three" value="math"><label>Math</label><input type="radio" name="three" value="system"><label>System</label><input type="radio" name="three" value="graphic"><label>Graphic</label> </p><p><h1>3)Frame package is in which package</h1><input type="radio" name="four" value="awt"><label>AWT</label><input type="radio" name="four" value="applet"><label>Applet</label><input type="radio" name="four" value="lang"><label>Lang</label><input type="radio" name="four" value="swing"><label>Swing</label></p><p><h1>4)Java does not support</h1><input type="radio" name="five" value="inheritance"><label>Inheritance</label><input type="radio" name="five" value="multiple inheritance"><label>Multiple inheritance</label><input type="radio" name="five" value="polymorphism"><label>Polymorphism</label><input type="radio" name="five" value="encryption"><label>Encryption</label></p><p><center><input type="submit" value="Submit"><input type ="reset" value="Reset"></center></p></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGSP.jpg)// 9\. JavaScript code to create a simple calculator<html><script type=text/javascript src=javascriptcode.js></script><body><table border><tr ><td colspan=4><input type=text id=res size=16 onfocus="this.blur();"></td></tr><tr><td><input type=button id=b1 value=0 size=2 onclick=f('0')></td><td><input type=button id=b2 value=1 size=2 onclick=f('1')></td><td><input type=button id=b3 value=2 size=2 onclick=f('2')></td><td><input type=button id=b4 value=+ size=2 onclick=f('+')></td></tr><tr><td><input type=button id=b5 value=3 size=2 onclick=f('3')></td><td><input type=button id=b6 value=4 size=2 onclick=f('4')></td><td><input type=button id=b7 value=5 size=2 onclick=f('5')></td><td><input type=button id=b8 value=- size=2 onclick=f('-')></td></tr><tr><td><input type=button id=b9 value=6 size=2 onclick=f('6')></td><td><input type=button id=b10 value=7 size=2 onclick=f('7')></td><td><input type=button id=b11 value=8 size=2 onclick=f('8')></td><td><input type=button id=b12 value=* size=2 onclick=f('*')></td></tr><tr><td><input type=button id=b13 value=9 size=2 onclick=f('9')></td><td><input type=button id=b14 value=+/- size=2 onclick=f('--')></td><td><input type=button id=b15 value='=' size=2 onclick=f('=')></td><td><input type=button id=b16 value='/' size=2 onclick=f('/')></td></tr><tr><td colspan=4 ><input type=button value=Clear size=16 onclick=f('c')></td></tr></table></body></html>Save this file with .html extension and the code below save it as javascriptcode.jsfunction f(d){if(d=='c'){document.getElementById('res').value="";return;}res=document.getElementById('res').value;if(res==0 && d==0)return;if(d=='+' || d=='-' || d=='*' || d=='/'){opr=d;num=parseFloat(res);document.getElementById('res').value=d;return;}if(d=='='){num1=parseFloat(res);switch(opr){case '+': ans=num+num1; break;case '-': ans=num-num1; break;case '*': ans=num*num1; break;case '/': ans=parseInt(num/num1); break;}document.getElementById('res').value=ans;return;}if(d=='--'){document.getElementById('res').value*=-1;return;}if(!isNaN(document.getElementById('res').value))document.getElementById('res').value+=d;elsedocument.getElementById('res').value=d;}Output:![](image_rsrcGSR.jpg)/* 10\. JavaScript code to display the content depending on the range selected by the user. */<html><head><title>Selecting table range</title></head><script type=text/javascript src=javascriptcode2.js></script><body onload=sc()></body></html>Save this code with .html extension and the code below save it as javascritptcode2.jsfunction sc(){rng=prompt('Enter the range');res=rng.split("-");if(res.length!=2){alert("invalid range ");return;}first=parseInt(res[0]);second=parseInt(res[1]);if(first>second){alert("invalid range ");return;}str="<table border=2><tr><th>Number</th><th>Square</th><th>Cube</th></tr>";for(i=first;i<=second;i++){str=str+"<tr><td>"+i+"<td>"+(i*i)+"<td>"+(i*i*i);}document.write(str);}Output:![](image_rsrcGSS.jpg)![](image_rsrcGST.jpg)// 11\. JavaScript code to reverse a number.<html><head><title>Reversing a number</title></head><meta charset="UTF-8"><title>Demonstrating Java scriptfunctions</title> </head><script type=text/javascript src=javascriptcode3.js></script><body><section><h1>Reverse of a number</h1><p>Enter a number: <input type=text id=t2></p><input type=button value=Reverseonclick=alert(reverse(document.getElementById('t2').value))></section></body></html>Save this file with .html extension and the code below save it as javascriptcode3.jsfunction reverse(num){rnum=0;temp=num;if(isNaN(num)){return "invalid number";}while(num!=0){rnum *= 10;rnum+= num % 10;num -= num % 10;num = Math.floor(num/10);}return "Reverse of num "+ temp + " is " + rnum;}Output:![](image_rsrcGSU.jpg)// 12\. JavaScript code to display numbers in words.<script language="javascript">function convert_number_to_words(number){hyphen = '-';conjunction = ' and ';separator = ', ';dictionary = {0 : 'zero',1 : 'one',2 : 'two',3 : 'three',4 : 'four',5 : 'five',6 : 'six',7 : 'seven',8 : 'eight',9 : 'nine',10 : 'ten',11 : 'eleven',12 : 'twelve',13 : 'thirteen',14 : 'fourteen',15 : 'fifteen',16 : 'sixteen',17 : 'seventeen',18 : 'eighteen',19 : 'nineteen',20 : 'twenty',30 : 'thirty',40 : 'fourty',50 : 'fifty',60 : 'sixty',70 : 'seventy',80 : 'eighty',90 : 'ninety',100 : 'hundred',};if (number < 0 || number > 999){alert("Enter a number range between 0 and 999");return "";}switch (true){case (number < 21):string = dictionary[number];break;case (number < 100):tens = parseInt(number / 10) * 10;units = number % 10;string = dictionary[tens];if (units) {string += hyphen + dictionary[units];}break;case (number < 1000):hundreds = parseInt(number / 100);remainder = number % 100;string = dictionary[hundreds] + ' ' + dictionary[100];if (remainder) {string += conjunction + convert_number_to_words(remainder);}break;default:break;}return string;}a=prompt("Enter a number");num=parseInt(a);document.write(convert_number_to_words(num));</script>Note: save this code with .html extensionOutput:![](image_rsrcGSV.jpg)![](image_rsrcGSW.jpg)// 13\. JavaScript code to display a multiplication table.<html><head><title> Multiplication Table </title></head><body><script type="text/javascript"><!--var n=prompt("Enter positive value for n: "," ");if(!isNaN(n)) {var table="";var number="";for(i=1;i<=10;i++) {number = n * i;table += n + " * " + i + " = " + number + "\n";}alert(table);}else {alert("Enter positive value");n=prompt("Enter positive value for n: "," ");}document.write(n+" table values displayed using alert ..<br />");// --></script></body></html>Output:![](image_rsrcGSX.jpg)![](image_rsrcGSY.jpg)// 14\. JavaScript code to find the factorial of a number.<html>  <head><title>Factorial of a number</title></head> <body>  Enter a number: <input id = "num"> <br><br>  <button onclick = "fact()"> Factorial </button> <p id = "sample"></p> <script>  function fact(){  var i, num, fact; fact = 1; num = document.getElementById("num").value; for(i = 1; i <= num; i++)   {  fact = fact * i; }  i=i-1;document.getElementById("sample").innerHTML = "The factorial of the number " + i + " is: " + fact ; }  </script>  </body>  </html>  Output:![](image_rsrcGSZ.jpg)// 15\. JavaScript code for registration form validation.<html><head><title>Registration Form Validation</title></head><body bgcolor="#E4F0F8"><script type='text/javascript'>function formValidator(){var firstname = document.getElementById('firstname');var lastname = document.getElementById('lastname');var email = document.getElementById('email');var pass = document.getElementById('pass');var addr = document.getElementById('addr');var mobileno = document.getElementById('mobileno');// Check each input in the order that it appears in the form!if(notEmpty(firstname, "can not be null")){if(isAlphabet(firstname, "Please enter only letters for your Firstname")){if(lengthRestriction(firstname, 6)){if(isAlphabet(lastname, "Please enter only letters for your Lastname")){if(emailValidator(email, "Please enter a valid email address")){if(lengthRestriction(pass, 6)){if(isAlphanumeric(pass, "please enter Numbers and Letters Only for password")){if(notEmpty(addr, "please enter the address")){if(isNumeric(mobileno, "Please enter a valid mobileno")){if(lengthRestriction1(mobileno, 10 , 10)){return true;} } } }}}}} } }return false;}function notEmpty(elem, helperMsg){if(elem.value.length == 0){alert(helperMsg);elem.focus(); // set the focus to this inputreturn false;}return true;}function isNumeric(elem, helperMsg){var numericExpression = /^[0-9]+$/;if(elem.value.match(numericExpression)){return true;}else{alert(helperMsg);elem.focus();return false;}}function isAlphabet(elem, helperMsg){var alphaExp = /^[a-zA-Z]+$/;if(elem.value.match(alphaExp)){return true;}else{alert(helperMsg);elem.focus();return false;}}function isAlphanumeric(elem, helperMsg){var alphaExp = /^[0-9a-zA-Z]+$/;if(elem.value.match(alphaExp)){return true;}else{alert(helperMsg);elem.focus();return false;}}function lengthRestriction(elem, min){var uInput = elem.value;if(uInput.length >= min){return true;}else{alert("Please enter minimum " +min+ " characters");elem.focus();return false;}}function emailValidator(elem, helperMsg){var emailExp = /^[\w\-\.\+]+\@[a-zA-Z0-9\.\-]+\.[a-zA-z0-9]{2,4}$/;if(elem.value.match(emailExp)){return true;}else{alert(helperMsg);elem.focus();return false;}}function lengthRestriction1(elem, min, max){var uInput = elem.value;if(uInput.length >= min && uInput.length <= max){return true;}else {alert("Please enter 10 numbers only");elem.focus();return false;}}</script><center><font color="blue" size="6" face="arial">Registration Form</font></center><br /><form onsubmit='return formValidator()' action="right.html">First Name(Minimum 6 characters)<font color="red">* </font><input type='text' id='firstname' /><br /><br />Last Name<font color="red"><font color="red">* </font> </font> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<input type='text' id='lastname' /><br /><br />Email Address<font color="red">* </font>
